| *(this commit)* | `b03f033d` + `4800ff54` + `74db2f3a` | **Amp and Codex re-anchored onto the chrome they paint today: OSC titles plus a status footer. No engine change and no Rust behavior change -- the only Rust edits are test fixtures.** Both fork manifests were byte-identical to their upstream parents, so each is taken as the whole upstream file state at its last commit in this group and is md5-identical to it (`amp.toml` `a1a29046e84462cf6effcb9f44165dce` at `b03f033d`, `codex.toml` `97ae03a81776207b6901bcd67b8ad200` at `74db2f3a`). **(1) amp `b03f033d`:** amp had only screen evidence, so a turn whose chrome was scrolled away read as idle. Four rules added -- `osc_title_plugin_confirmation_blocked` (blocked, 1100, `osc_title`, `contains ["Plugin confirmation needed"]`), `osc_title_working` (working, 1050, `osc_title`, `regex ['^[\x{2800}-\x{28FF}] ']`), `status_footer_working` (working, 200, `bottom_non_empty_lines(5)`, `line_regex ['(?i)^\s*╰\s+\S+\s+(thinking|streaming|running tools|waiting)\s+─']`) and `osc_title_idle` (idle, 50, `osc_title`, `contains [" - amp - "]` with `not` gates on the braille regex and the confirmation string). The existing `approval_footer` (300) and `esc_cancel_working` (100) are untouched. `min_engine_version` `1` -> `2` because the manifest now reads `osc_title`; the fork's `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` is already `2`, so no const moves and engine 3 stays M3-07's to claim. `version` `2026.06.10.1` -> `2026.07.09.1`. **(2) codex `4800ff54`:** Codex moved its braille spinner off the front of the OSC title, so the anchored `^[\x{2800}-\x{28FF}] ` missed it, `osc_title_idle`'s `not` gate missed it too, and a working pane reported Idle. Both sites switch to the unanchored explicit frame class `(?:^| )[⠋⠙⠹⠸⠼⠴⠦⠧⠇⠏](?: |$)`. This is a **re-shape, not a superset**: it is *narrower* in the code points it accepts (ten spinner frames instead of the whole braille block) and *wider* in where it accepts them (anywhere the frame is bounded by a space or an edge), which is upstream's intent. `version` -> `2026.07.13.1`. **(3) codex `74db2f3a`:** with a static title a working Codex had no evidence at all. New `screen_working_fallback` -- working, priority 500, `bottom_non_empty_lines(3)`, `line_regex ['^[•◦]\s+Working \([^)]*esc to interrupt\)(?: · .*)?$']`, `not [{ contains = ["■ Conversation interrupted"] }]`. Seated at 500 it sits below `transcript_viewer` (1000), `live_strong_blocker` (900) and `weak_blocker` (600) and above nothing else, so a live blocker or the transcript viewer still wins. `version` -> `2026.07.18.1`, `updated_at` -> `2026-07-18T00:00:00Z`. **Rust half of `4800ff54` -- remote-manifest test fixtures renumbered `2026.06.10.x` -> `9999.01.01.x`, adapted because the fork's fixtures sat one step lower than upstream's.** Upstream renumbered `.5`/`.6`; the fork carried `.3`/`.4` for the same roles, so the mapping is by role rather than by literal, and every literal now equals upstream's post-commit value byte-for-byte. Twelve in `src/detect/manifest/tests.rs` (`2026.06.10.3` -> `9999.01.01.1`, `2026.06.10.4` -> `9999.01.01.2`) across `remote_manifest_loads_between_local_override_and_bundled`, `fallback_explain_preserves_active_manifest_version`, `local_override_shadows_cached_remote_manifest`, `invalid_local_override_falls_back_to_cached_remote_manifest` and `detection_uses_cached_manifest_until_explicit_reload`; eight in `src/detect/manifest_update.rs`, all to `9999.01.01.1` except `process_agent_manifest_rejects_downgrade_and_keeps_cached_manifest`'s *older* probe, which becomes `9999.01.01.0` so it stays a downgrade. `older_cached_remote_manifest_does_not_shadow_newer_bundled_manifest` keeps `2026.06.10.0`: that fixture must be *older* than the bundled manifest and still is. **This is load-bearing, not housekeeping, in this commit specifically** -- the fixtures only worked while they outranked the bundled codex version, and item (3) pushes that version to `2026.07.18.1`. **Tests -- seven upstream, two fork-only.** The seven from `74db2f3a` are byte-identical to upstream and land in upstream's position, between `codex_background_terminal_screen_does_not_override_osc_idle` and `codex_osc_working_beats_weak_blocker_screen`: `codex_screen_working_fallback_handles_static_osc_title`, `codex_osc_working_remains_preferred_over_screen_fallback`, `codex_screen_blocker_outranks_working_fallback`, `codex_weak_blocker_outranks_working_fallback`, `codex_transcript_viewer_outranks_working_fallback`, `codex_screen_working_fallback_ignores_stale_and_prompt_text`, `codex_screen_working_fallback_ignores_interrupted_short_terminal`. Neither `b03f033d` nor `4800ff54` shipped a test, so two are written fresh here: `codex_osc_title_spinner_away_from_the_title_start_is_working` (a trailing and a mid-title frame both -> Working via `osc_title_working`; a braille cell welded inside a word is *not* a spinner and stays Idle) and `amp_manifest_detects_osc_states_and_the_status_footer` (confirmation title -> Blocked; braille title -> Working even though the title still carries `" - amp - "`, i.e. the idle `not` gate holds; all four footer verbs under a static title -> Working via `status_footer_working`, which is the 200-over-50 precedence; a static title with no footer -> Idle; a footer stranded above five newer lines -> Idle, pinning the `bottom_non_empty_lines(5)` bound). **Red/green proof, with attribution probes, so no assertion is left vacuous.** Reverting `codex.toml` to its pre-port parent turns exactly two tests red -- `codex_screen_working_fallback_handles_static_osc_title` and the new spinner test -- and reverting it only as far as `4800ff54` leaves just the first, which attributes each red to its own upstream commit. Reverting `amp.toml` turns the amp test red. The remaining five codex tests are ordering and negative guards that pass with or without the rule, so each was pinned separately against the shipped manifest: raising `screen_working_fallback` from 500 to 1200 turns all four ordering guards red (so the seating, not the rule's existence, is what they test); deleting its `not` gate turns `codex_screen_working_fallback_ignores_interrupted_short_terminal` red; unanchoring its `line_regex` from `^[•◦]` turns `codex_screen_working_fallback_ignores_stale_and_prompt_text` red. **One gap recorded rather than papered over:** widening the rule's region from `bottom_non_empty_lines(3)` to `whole_recent` turns *nothing* red -- upstream's three stale-text fixtures are all defeated by the `^[•◦]` anchor before the region is ever consulted, so the codex region bound ships untested upstream and here. The amp test's stale-footer case covers the equivalent bound on `amp.toml`. Every revert was restored and re-verified md5-identical. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `detect::` 100 -> 109 tests passing and `detect::manifest::tests::` 41 -> 50; the `codex_` filter 31/31 (24 -> 31) and `amp_manifest` 1/1; `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_agent_detection_manifest_check` 5/5; `python3 scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py --website-dir /nonexistent/agent-detection` prints `agent detection manifests ok`, which is the bundled half of the checker validating both refreshed files. **EXCLUDED:** the `website/agent-detection/{amp,codex}.toml` mirror hunks from all three commits (untracked, gitignored, byte-identical upstream to the bundled copy) and `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md` from `74db2f3a` (upstream release artifact). Nothing else is touched -- no engine code, no schema, no manifests beyond these two. **Licensing:** `b03f033d` (2026-07-09), `4800ff54` (2026-07-13) and `74db2f3a` (2026-07-18) all predate `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), so all three arrive under AGPL-3.0-or-later and **no path is appended to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list** (it stays at 6 entries). |
| *(this commit)* | `99760f77` *(engine half)* | **A new manifest region that reads the TOP of the buffer, and the engine bump that gates it.** Grok clears its OSC busy signals while a background task runs, so the only remaining evidence of work is an animated chip pinned in the *first* non-empty row of application chrome. Every region in the fork's vocabulary addresses the bottom of the buffer or a prompt-relative anchor (`src/detect/manifest.rs` `validate_region_name`), so that chip was unaddressable. `99760f77` adds `top_non_empty_lines(N)` -- the mirror image of `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)`, returning the buffer prefix through the N-th non-empty line from the top -- and moves the manifest engine to 3. **Four hunks in `src/detect/manifest.rs`, all upstream-verbatim** (the diff of the two Rust files is byte-identical to `99760f77`'s): the region name is accepted in `validate_region_name` via `top_region_count(trimmed).is_some()`; `region()` gains the matching dispatch arm; the new `top_non_empty_lines` slicer reuses the existing `line_start_offset` helper (no new imports, and `slice_from_line_index` is untouched); and `validate_manifest` gains a guard that rejects a `top_non_empty_lines` rule whose manifest declares a `min_engine_version` below `TOP_NON_EMPTY_LINES_ENGINE_VERSION`. **The region name is declared with its own validation const, not a bare literal:** `TOP_NON_EMPTY_LINES_ENGINE_VERSION: u32 = 3` and `MAX_TOP_REGION_LINE_COUNT: usize = u16::MAX as usize` (65,535) sit beside the new `top_region_count` parser, and both are mirrored on the Python side (`scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py`: `REGION_RE` gains a third alternative, the new `REGION_COUNT_RE` catches an in-syntax count that is numerically too large, `MAX_TOP_REGION_LINE_COUNT = 65_535`, plus the two rejections in `validate_manifest` and `validate_rule`). **`top_region_count` is deliberately stricter than the existing `region_count` and does not replace it:** it rejects a leading `0`, a `+` sign, any non-ASCII-digit byte and any count above 65,535, whereas `region_count` still accepts `bottom_lines(0)` and `bottom_lines(+1)`. Retrofitting the strictness onto `region_count` would change how every existing `bottom_lines(N)` / `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)` spec parses and would desync the Rust matcher from `REGION_RE`, which is unbounded on digit count for those two names -- upstream left the asymmetry in place and so does this commit. **Why the const bump is safe on its own here.** The `min_engine_version > MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` rejection lives in `parse_remote_manifest_for_agent` (`src/detect/manifest.rs:884`) and therefore fires only on *remote* manifests; the new `top_non_empty_lines` guard is the opposite -- it sits inside `validate_manifest` and so fires on *bundled* files too. No bundled manifest declares 3 or uses the new region yet, and a bundled manifest only ever needs `min_engine_version` at or below the const, so raising 2 -> 3 cannot invalidate one: `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate` stays green and the bundled half of the checker (`python3 scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py --website-dir /nonexistent/agent-detection`) prints `agent detection manifests ok`. The reverse split *would* be unsafe, which is why `grok.toml`'s 2 -> 3 bump ships together with its `background_work_chip_working` rule in M3-08 rather than here. **This is the only `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` bump in M3.** **Red/green proof, one mutation per hunk, each restored md5-identical afterwards.** Dropping the `top_region_count` arm from `validate_region_name` reds only `top_non_empty_lines_requires_a_canonical_positive_bounded_count`; dropping the `region()` dispatch arm reds only `top_non_empty_lines_uses_top_occurrence_for_repeated_text`; dropping the `validate_manifest` guard reds only `top_non_empty_lines_requires_engine_three_when_declared`. On the Python side, dropping the `validate_manifest` rejection reds `test_rejects_top_non_empty_lines_below_engine_three`, dropping the `REGION_COUNT_RE` bound reds the two over-bound subtests of `test_top_non_empty_lines_requires_canonical_positive_bounded_count`, and dropping the `REGION_RE` alternative reds both. **Two gaps recorded rather than papered over.** (1) The const bump itself is unpinned: reverting `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` to 2 leaves `detect::` at 112/112 green, because every new test goes through `parse_manifest`, which never consults the const. It first becomes load-bearing in M3-08, when `grok.toml` declares `min_engine_version = 3` and the remote path would otherwise reject it. (2) `top_non_empty_lines_requires_engine_three_when_declared` asserts only `parse_manifest(..).is_err()`, not the message, so it also passes under a broken region acceptance (it survived mutation A above) -- it pins the guard's existence, not its reason. Both shapes are upstream's. **EXCLUDED, with reasons.** From `scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py`: the `import hashlib`, the `STAGED_WEBSITE_MANIFESTS` allowlist and the `staged_manifest` / `website_digest` / `stages_new_engine_manifest` branch in `validate_catalog`. Upstream uses them to let one published website manifest lag the bundled one while engine-2 clients catch up, keyed on a sha256 of the website file. `/website/` is gitignored here and the fork publishes no remote catalog, so there is no engine-2 client population to protect and no website file to digest -- carrying the allowlist would pin a checksum of an untracked file, passing on this machine and failing in CI. `validate_catalog` is left unchanged. The three staged-website tests are excluded for the same reason (`staged_grok_dirs` reads `check.DEFAULT_WEBSITE_DIR/"grok.toml"`, which no CI checkout has); the two tests taken are synthetic and keep the fork's Python suite free of any `DEFAULT_WEBSITE_DIR` dependency. Also excluded: `src/detect/manifests/grok.toml` (the manifest half, which lands in M3-08 with `b04c6496` and `b42c6e66`) and the `website/agent-detection/grok.toml` mirror. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `detect::` 109 -> 112 passing and `detect::manifest::tests::` 50 -> 53; the `top_non_empty_lines` filter 3/3 and `manifest_update` 13/13; `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_agent_detection_manifest_check` 5 -> 7 OK; the bundled checker run above ok; `python3 scripts/check_public_tree.py` clean (1671 files). *(Running the checker with no `--website-dir` override fails on this machine -- the local gitignored `website/agent-detection/amp.toml` still carries `2026.06.10.1` against the bundled `2026.07.09.1` from M3-06. That is a stale untracked mirror, not a regression: the HEAD copy of the script fails identically, and `just check` only ever runs the unittest, never the script's catalog path.)* **This row also corrects the M3-06 (a05ce3b) row: the `codex_` filter count was 23 -> 31, not 24 -> 31** (existing rows are never edited). **Licensing:** `99760f77` is dated 2026-07-17 and predates `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), so it arrives under AGPL-3.0-or-later and **no path is appended to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list** (it stays at 6 entries). |
| *(this commit)* | `b04c6496` + `b42c6e66` + `99760f77` *(manifest half)* | **Grok detection rebuilt end to end: current UI chrome, then terminal signals, then the pinned background-work chip.** The fork's `grok.toml` was byte-identical to upstream `v0.7.1` (md5 `472c8ded09d2d8bcbeed043d482f8629`) and carried two rules written for Grok Build 0.1.x: a scope-selector blocker and a braille-spinner working rule. Upstream rewrote the file in three commits and **nothing else in `v0.7.1..v0.8.2` touches it** (`git log v0.8.2 -- src/detect/manifests/grok.toml`), so the whole file is taken as upstream's state at `99760f77` -- md5 `683e9a6532260a20d54b24aef7b940ee`, byte-identical to `v0.8.2:src/detect/manifests/grok.toml` -- rather than replayed as three diffs; the fork copy differs from that state in exactly one line. `version` 2026.06.10.1 -> 2026.07.16.2, `min_engine_version` **1 -> 3**, rules 2 -> 13, `aliases` unchanged (`grok-build`). Grok carries no `zynk:` alias line, so the rebrand class that applies to `kilo`/`opencode`/`pi` does not apply here. **`b04c6496` (2026-07-06) re-anchors on Grok Build 0.2.82 pane chrome:** six new rules -- `option_dialog_blocked` (320, `whole_recent`, a `line_regex` over the gutter-drawn option list), `permission_hints_blocked` (310) and `question_dialog_hints_blocked` (305) over `bottom_non_empty_lines(2)` footer hints, `spinner_status_working` (200, a braille spinner line ending in the `[stop]` chip), `esc_cancel_hints_working` (190) and `prompt_hints_idle` (100, gated `not` on either cancel hint) -- plus one edit to the surviving `waiting_tool_working`: its braille class moves from the four-digit escape `[⠀-⣿]` to `[\x{2801}-\x{28FF}]`, which is both a syntax normalisation and a real narrowing, since dropping U+2800 (the blank braille cell the startup splash paints its logo with) stops a splash row reading as a running tool. Its priority stays 120; **the port sheet's §2 C3 table lists it as 250, which is wrong** -- `b04c6496` changes only the pattern, and 120 is the value in both `v0.7.1` and `v0.8.2`. The `min_engine_version` 1 -> 2 bump in this commit is pre-emptive: `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)` is an engine-1 region, and the OSC rules that actually need engine 2 arrive in the next commit. **`b42c6e66` (2026-07-17) makes Grok OSC-first:** Grok Build 0.2.101 emits OSC 0 titles and OSC 9;4 progress, so five rules land -- `osc_title_blocked` (1300, `Action Required`), `osc_progress_working` (1150, `^4;1;-1$`), `osc_title_idle` (1100, the `grok` title anchor, gated `not` on any braille frame), `osc_title_working` (1000, a bare non-whitespace catch-all) and `osc_progress_idle` (950, `^4;0;0$`) -- and **every screen rule is re-seated into the 1180-1300 band** (320 -> 1200, 310 -> 1190, 305 -> 1185, 300 -> 1180). The re-seat is load-bearing, not cosmetic: once `osc_title_working` matches any title that is not a known idle one, a permission prompt under a custom title would outrank every screen blocker at its old priority and report *working*. The header comment is rewritten with the new evidence. **`99760f77` (2026-07-17) closes the background-work hole:** Grok clears both OSC signals while a background task runs, leaving only an animated task-count chip in the first non-empty row of pinned chrome, so `background_work_chip_working` (1170, `top_non_empty_lines(1)`) lands and `min_engine_version` goes 2 -> 3. That rule is the reason M3-07 had to precede this commit: the `top_non_empty_lines` guard sits inside `validate_manifest` and fires on bundled files, so a bundled `grok.toml` declaring 2 while carrying the rule would fail validation outright, and one declaring 3 against `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION = 2` would be rejected on the remote path. **The single adaptation:** the `b42c6e66` header comment names the upstream product as the thing that retains the payload after `9;`; that sentence is rewritten to name zynk, because ADR 0010 §7's CI gate bans the upstream product name in active source including comments. Nothing else in the file differs from upstream. **Tests are fork-authored -- all three upstream commits ship zero.** Six tests in `src/detect/manifest/tests.rs`, after the Amp section: `grok_option_dialog_and_footer_hints_are_blocked` (the gutter list wins at 1200 over the permission footer at 1190; each footer rule answers alone when the list is not drawn; note `Esc:unselect` does not contain the `:select` needle, so the two footer rules cannot answer for each other; a footer stranded above two newer lines is stale scrollback and matches nothing); `grok_spinner_status_and_cancel_hints_are_working` (the `[stop]`-anchored status line; the cancel footer beating `prompt_hints_idle` through its `not` gate; and the U+2800 narrowing, where a blank-braille splash row must not read as a running tool while a real spinner frame does); `grok_prompt_hints_are_idle_only_without_a_cancel_hint`; `grok_osc_title_and_progress_carry_the_turn_state` (both idle title forms, a spinner title falling through the braille `not` gate to the catch-all, the `Action Required` title needing its 1300 seat because the blink-on title still ends in the idle anchor, both progress payloads, and an unrelated payload matching neither); `grok_screen_blockers_outrank_the_generic_non_idle_title` (all four re-seated blockers under a custom title); `grok_background_work_chip_is_working_from_the_pinned_top_row` (the chip, a zero count, and the same chip one row lower). **Red/green proof, three layers, the manifest restored md5-identical after each.** (1) Whole-file: the six new tests against the `v0.7.1` `grok.toml` fail 6/6. (2) Per-rule ablation: dropping each of the twelve rules in turn reds exactly the test that names it -- `osc_title_blocked`, `osc_progress_working`, `osc_title_idle`, `osc_title_working` and `osc_progress_idle` red the OSC test; `option_dialog_blocked` reds both the blocker test and the re-seat test; `permission_hints_blocked` and `question_dialog_hints_blocked` red the blocker test; `spinner_status_working` and `esc_cancel_hints_working` red the working test; `prompt_hints_idle` reds the idle test; `background_work_chip_working` reds the chip test. (3) Per-edit ablation: reverting the braille class to `[\x{2800}-\x{28FF}]` reds only the blank-braille assertion; reverting `option_dialog_blocked` to 320 reds the re-seat test with `left: Working, right: Blocked` -- the exact regression the band exists to prevent -- and also flips the blocker test's winner to `permission_hints_blocked`; reverting `permission_scope_selector` to 300, or the two footer rules to 310/305, reds the re-seat test on that entry alone. **EXCLUDED:** `website/agent-detection/grok.toml` (all three commits; `/website/` is gitignored and the fork ships only the bundled copy) and `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md` (`b04c6496`; private upstream release artifact). **Licensing:** `b04c6496` is dated 2026-07-06 and `b42c6e66` / `99760f77` 2026-07-17, all before `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), so they arrive under AGPL-3.0-or-later and **no path is appended to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list** (it stays at 6 entries). **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `detect::` 112 -> 118 passing and `detect::manifest::tests::` 53 -> 59; the `grok` filter 6/6; `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate` 1/1 with grok now at `min_engine_version = 3`; `manifest_update` 13/13 unchanged; `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_agent_detection_manifest_check` 7/7 OK; `python3 scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py --website-dir /nonexistent/agent-detection` prints `agent detection manifests ok`; `python3 scripts/check_public_tree.py` clean (1671 files) and `python3 scripts/scrub_check.py` clean (82 scoped files). *(As recorded in the M3-07 row, running the checker with no `--website-dir` override still fails on this machine -- the local gitignored `website/agent-detection/` mirror now lags on eight manifests including `grok.toml`. It is a stale untracked mirror, not a regression: `just check` only ever runs the unittest, never the script's catalog path.)* |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix)* | **Claude's current-approval detection becomes structural and wrap-aware, closing the OPEN Gate-3 finding `ARB-18C-BOTTOM-WINDOW-001`** (major; it de-duplicates the five helper reports `SENT-R11-DETECT-001/002`, `AUD-18C-FRESH-001/002`, `INSPECTOR-18C-001`, `ARCH-18C316-OSC-TAIL-001` and `WARDEN-18C316-FRESHNESS-001`; evidence carried in `probe-boundaries.log` + `probe-terminal-wrap-evaluator.log`). The checkpoint-0 rule `current_approval_dialog` (990) selected an unconditional two-row PHYSICAL suffix, which is neither the documented contract nor wrap-aware: (1) an answered unboxed footer with exactly ONE later work row stayed inside the window and kept reporting Blocked, and (2) below roughly 48 columns the terminal soft-wraps the footer, so `esc to cancel` split across rows or fell out of the window and the waiting approval read as Working. Both directions are reproduced first as failing tests on the production path. **Engine:** `DetectionInput` gains `unwrapped_tail: Option<&str>`, the soft-wrap-joined twin of the same bottom-of-buffer snapshot, threaded from the two pane detection loops and the `agent explain` handler via a new `detection_unwrapped_text()` that shares `ghostty_detection_rows()` with `detection_text()` — still a snapshot, never the parser or the viewport. New region `bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(N)` selects the last N non-empty LOGICAL lines from that tail and falls back to the physical `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)` when no tail is supplied (remote and shim callers). It rides the existing engine-3 floor: `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` stays 3 (bumped once in M3 by `99760f77`), and the per-region gate + strict canonical count parser are generalized over `ENGINE_3_REGIONS` in both `src/detect/manifest.rs` and `scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py`. **Manifest:** `claude.toml` 2026.09.09.1, `min_engine_version = 3`; `current_approval_dialog` splits into `current_approval_footer` (990, `bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(1)`, the existing footer grammar) and `current_approval_boxed` (990, `bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(2)`, the same grammar plus a closing-box-border regex anchored with `\z` to the end of the region, so a boxed dialog admits its earlier footer row only under PROVEN chrome). Every other rule and priority is unchanged. **Tests:** a production-path matrix rendered through the real Ghostty terminal at 80/40/30/20 columns × boxed/unboxed × 0/1/2 post-answer work rows × half-circle and Braille titles, asserting the winning rule id and the visibility flags through `detection_update_for_publish_with_osc` and `explain_with_input`; the `SENT-R11-DETECT-003` negative control (footer grammar quoted inside a log line is not a dialog); a static-title prompt-box control (Idle via `live_prompt_box`); the logical-region unit tests and their engine-3 gate in both languages. The six fork-only precedence tests stay green, with the boxed and unboxed fixtures now naming the rule that must claim each. **Docs:** the CHANGELOG entry states the exact supported contract in place of the checkpoint-0 overclaim. **NOT touched:** the live override at `~/.config/zynk/agent-detection/claude.toml` (still 2026.09.08.4 / engine 2) — rolling it out is a separate operator gate. |

Upstream seats it at 975, between its `live_blocked_form` (980) and `live_prompt_box` (950); the fork compressed that band (busy title 970, blocked forms 960, prompt box 950), so a literal 975 would have jumped the fork's entire blocked band. **955 reproduces both upstream relations** — a genuinely blocked form (960) still wins, the idle prompt box (950) still loses — and cannot collide with the 990 current-dialog rules, whose mandatory token is `esc to cancel` while the overlay footer says `Esc to close`. **`4473e391` — `live_blocked_form`'s grammar restructured, priority kept at 960.** Claude's newer confirmation prints `Enter to confirm` with no option list and no navigate hint, so the mandatory `contains = ["enter to select", "esc to cancel"]` never matched and a blocked Claude read as idle. `esc to cancel` becomes the only mandatory token and the discriminating evidence moves into `any`: a bare `enter to confirm`, or `enter to select` **nested with** an `any` over the five navigate-hint variants. **Adaptations.** (1) *The 990 rules stay as M3-09 left them* — `current_approval_footer` and `current_approval_boxed` already list `enter to confirm` in their `any`, and they read a different region (`bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(1\|2)` vs `after_last_horizontal_rule`), so both halves are needed: the 990 rules claim a footer that is still the logical tail, `live_blocked_form` claims one that has scrolled above it. (2) *Their bare `{ contains = ["to navigate"] }` is deliberately NOT propagated* into `live_blocked_form`: `after_last_horizontal_rule` is a suffix selector, not proof of freshness, so upstream's explicit five-variant list is what keeps stale scrollback from matching. (3) Upstream's `version` / `updated_at` hunks are excluded (the fork is ahead of `2026.08.04.1`), and `website/agent-detection/claude.toml` is excluded throughout — that tree is not tracked here. **Recorded, not re-applied:** `9ba32c25` (half-circle busy frames `U+25D0..U+25D1`) and `2075de5d` (widened to `U+25D0..U+25D3`, plus a test) are **no-ops for this fork** — `osc_title_working` already carries the `\x{25D0}-\x{25D3}` superset with an equivalent explaining comment, and `claude_osc_title_half_circle_frames_are_working` already covers the same four frames with the same three assertions. Verified green, nothing changed. **Tests:** four new in `src/detect/manifest/tests.rs` — the `/btw` overlay reports Working under both an empty and a static `✳` title, with each `line_regex` half proved load-bearing on its own; a confirmation after a divider is Blocked through both grammar branches (option list plus navigate hint, and the bare `enter to confirm` this commit exists for); an `enter to select` remnant with no navigate hint is NOT claimed by `live_blocked_form`, with the hint restored as the positive control; and the 955 seat is proved in both directions from `evaluated_rules` (loses to `live_blocked_form` at 960 while itself matching, beats `live_prompt_box` at 950 while that itself matches). The six fork-only precedence tests and the five M3-09 production-path width-matrix tests in `src/pane/terminal.rs` stay green. **Licensing:** `4473e391` is dated 2026-08-04, after `cd5ea1be`, so `src/detect/manifests/claude.toml` is appended to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list; `a47bc3e0` (2026-07-13) is pre-relicense and adds nothing there. **NOT touched:** the live override at `~/.config/zynk/agent-detection/claude.toml` — rolling a manifest out to the live runtime stays a separate operator gate. |
| *(this commit)* | `5f1957c2` *(detect half; asset half deferred to M4)* | **Hermes is demoted from lifecycle hook authority to a session-identity-only integration, and its screen manifest is hardened to carry the lifecycle instead.** Hermes's hook integration reports session identity only, never lifecycle transitions, so leaving it in `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` let a stale identity report pin the pane to a state the screen contradicted. `src/detect/mod.rs` drops `("zynk:hermes", "hermes")` from `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` and adds `session_identity_only_integration(source, agent_label)` (the upstream `herdr:` source labels rebranded to `zynk:` per ADR 0010 §7). `src/detect/manifests/hermes.toml` is replaced wholesale with upstream's post-commit file -- the fork copy was byte-identical to upstream's parent (md5 `b28b6c24bf116182482f39ba179d49d4`) and carries no fork alias, so this is a verbatim take: `min_engine_version` 1 -> 2, version `2026.06.10.1` -> `2026.07.24.1`, and the rule table grows from two whole-pane rules to nine bounded ones -- OSC-title `osc_title_blocked` (1100), `osc_title_working` (1050) and `osc_title_idle` (100); `interrupt_status_working` moved off `whole_recent` priority 100 onto `bottom_non_empty_lines(5)` at 950 with `ctrl+c to interrupt` alongside `msg=interrupt`; the new `classic_cancel_working` (500) on the same window; and four blocked panels at 900 on `bottom_non_empty_lines(14)` -- `dangerous_command_approval` (moved off `whole_recent` priority 300, gates widened), plus the new `clarification_prompt`, `credential_prompt` and `confirmation_prompt`, each with explicit `all`/`any` gates so a bounded footer, not incidental scrollback, decides the state. **ADAPTED, `src/terminal/state.rs`** (taken here, not deferred, so the demotion ships with its consumer and no `#[allow(dead_code)]` is needed): the early return guards the fork's single hook-authority entry point `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at`, which is where the fork funnels the whole `set_hook_authority*` family, rather than upstream's `set_hook_authority_at`; the session-replacement arm `("zynk:hermes", "hermes", Some("startup" | "new" | "resume"))` is appended to `session_start_source_allows_session_replacement` (the fork's list has three arms where upstream has six -- the mastracode/pi/omp arms come from commits outside this port range); and the replacement guard is recomputed against the fork's simpler `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start`, which has neither upstream's `process_present`/`session_replacement_allowed` locals nor its `recent_agent_process_exit` field (a later upstream commit, out of range). The local is therefore named `replacing_identity_only_session` after the generic predicate rather than upstream's hermes-specific `replacing_hermes_session`, and `process_present` reduces to `parse_agent_label(agent_label)` matching `detected_agent` -- the fork clears a persisted session on process exit inside `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at`, so the dropped `recent_agent_process_exit` clause has no fork equivalent to lose. Net behavior matches upstream: an identity-only integration may replace its own anchored id-kind session only while its process is the detected agent, so a background or stale start report cannot silently repoint the pane's identity. **Tests:** new `detect::tests::hermes_session_integration_leaves_state_to_screen_detection` (rebranded) and `terminal::state::tests::hermes_session_claim_leaves_state_to_detection` (upstream's, adapted to the fork's `set_hook_authority_with_session_ref` custom-status parameter and its `TerminalStateMutation` without `agent_released`); `visible_working_does_not_override_full_lifecycle_hook_idle` and `detected_agent_disappearance_does_not_clear_full_lifecycle_hook_session_ref` re-anchor from Hermes to Kimi, exactly as upstream did, because they need an agent that still holds full-lifecycle authority. **EXCLUDED -- deferred to M4:** the integration-asset half (`src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py`, `scripts/test_hermes_integration_asset.py`, the `justfile` recipe, and upstream's removal of the eight `HERMES_PLUGIN_INIT_ASSET` assertions in `src/integration/tests.rs`) -- the fork's asset is unchanged here, so those assertions still describe it and stay green. **EXCLUDED:** `website/agent-detection/hermes.toml` (the `website/` tree is untracked in this fork). That last one is the non-obvious hunk: Qwen renames its main thread, so `argv0`/`name` is `MainThread` -- neither an agent nor a generic runtime -- and the existing `is_generic_runtime_or_shell` branch never fires. Reading the runtime back out of `argv[0]` fixes it, and upstream's narrowing to `identify_agent(..) == Some(Agent::Qwen)` is kept verbatim: widening it would let any renamed process claim an identity the earlier branches deliberately refused. **TAKEN, `src/detect/manifest.rs`:** the `("qwen", include_str!("manifests/qwen.toml"))` entry (`:265`). **TAKEN, `src/config/sound.rs`:** the `qwen` field (`:46`), the `Some(Agent::Qwen)` arm of the exhaustive `for_agent` match (`:142`) and the `Default` arm (`:182`). **TAKEN, doc lists:** `cjk_ime_agents`' accepted names gain `qwen, qwen-code` in `src/config/model.rs:944` and in the shipped default config template `src/main.rs:351-352` (the fork's template line does not list `devin`, so it is reflowed on its own wording rather than upstream's). **Tests:** `detect::tests::identify_agent_in_job_detects_node_wrapped_qwen` (upstream's, verbatim -- the fnm shim path and the npm package entry point, both behind a `MainThread` process name) plus the qwen assertions appended to `identify_known_agents` and `parse_known_agent_labels`; Those two are a single hook policy and are held back as a pair: no `zynk:qwen` source can exist until the integration is ported, so landing half of the policy would declare a contract for a reporter that cannot report. Qwen loses nothing in the meantime -- it has a screen manifest, and screen detection is exactly what the deferred policy would route it to. **EXCLUDED -- no fork counterpart:** `src/config/sidebar.rs` (the fork has no sidebar module) and upstream's `interactive_agent_executable` arm (no such function here). **EXCLUDED -- untracked/private:** `website/agent-detection/qwen.toml`, `website/agent-detection/index.toml`, `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/next/api/*.schema.json` and the fifteen `docs/next/website/**` pages. Upstream `a4d52ab6` (2026-08-13) and `9e6c2b4e` (2026-08-14) are both after `cd5ea1be`, so `NOTICE` §4(b) gains `src/config/model.rs`, `src/config/sound.rs`, `src/detect/manifest.rs`, `src/detect/manifests/qwen.toml` (recorded even though it is a new file rather than a modified one -- it is upstream Apache-2.0 content end to end) and `src/main.rs` (`src/detect/mod.rs` was already listed). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `5f1957c2` row above)* | **Hook IDENTITY is split from lifecycle AUTHORITY, so a session-identity-only integration keeps the identity it reports.** Codex Gate-2 M3 finding (`msg_34f2e9b655927aaf`, P2): the row above routed `session_identity_only_integration` reports to an early `return None` in the fork's single hook-report funnel `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` (`src/terminal/state.rs`), which discarded the WHOLE report -- including the `agent_session_id` the still-bundled Hermes asset sends through `pane.report_agent` (`src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py`). `pane.get` then lost the session, and `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs`), which required `terminal.hook_authority`, answered `receiver_identity_unverified` for a really-detected Hermes pane that had explicitly reported its session. **Fix.** `TerminalState` gains `hook_identity: Option<HookIdentity { source, agent_label }>` beside `hook_authority`. The identity-only branch of the funnel now calls the new `record_identity_only_hook_report`, which keeps the hook-derived identity (source, label and session) under the same seq, detected-agent-conflict, owner-coherence and same-owner session-replacement clamp the full-lifecycle path applies, and drops only the lifecycle half: `state`/`message`/`custom_status` are ignored and `hook_authority` is never taken, so no state arbitration runs and the screen stays the sole lifecycle authority. `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` records the same identity for these integrations, because for them the session report IS the identity report. The identity ends with its process: dropped on the same process-exit evidence that drops the session, when the detected agent contradicts the reported label, on that owner's `clear`/`release` (another owner's leaves it alone, and the release path now also matches on the identity alone), and on respawn. `authoritative_receiver_identity` resolves the label from `hook_authority` OR `hook_identity` with owner coherence unchanged; a detection-only pane has neither and stays `receiver_identity_unverified`. The third consumer in the chain, the App-side `HookStateReported` dispatch (`src/app/actions.rs`), now names the identity-only shape explicitly beside the existing `is_reserved_native_state_source` branch and calls `record_identity_only_hook_report` directly, so the routing is visible where the report arrives instead of hidden behind a method named for hook authority; `TerminalState` keeps the same guard inside the funnel, so a caller reaching it directly still cannot route around the split. Reserved-native sources (claude, codex, copilot, devin, droid, qodercli, cursor) are deliberately NOT given hook identity: they persist a session and stay non-receipt-capable exactly as before. **Deliberately unchanged** because they are lifecycle, not identity: `full_lifecycle_hook_authority_active` and its callers (`src/app/agents.rs`, `src/app/api/agents.rs`, `src/app/api.rs`, `src/app/actions.rs` screen-detection skip), `effective_agent_label`/`effective_known_agent` and the effective-state arbitration in `src/terminal/state.rs`, and the hook-authority `custom_status` + metadata `applies_to_source` guards in `src/terminal/metadata.rs`. `terminal_agent_session_info` (`src/app/creation.rs`) and the snapshot capture (`src/persist/snapshot.rs`) need no change: both already fall back to `persisted_agent_session`, which is where the identity-only session lands. `src/zynk/header.rs::is_agent_target` likewise needs none -- the restored `agent_session` satisfies it. **Tests.** Eight `terminal::state` unit tests (identity kept without authority, identity without a session, the replacement clamp, process exit, conflicting detected agent, owner-scoped clear/release, and pi still taking authority with no identity); one `app::actions` characterization test, the sibling of `reserved_native_state_report_does_not_override_screen_state`, pinning the new dispatch branch; and five `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` integration tests, two of them the reviewer's probes (`codex-m3-hermes-probes.patch`) adapted to the fork harness. The five cover the SHIPPED reporter path (one `pane.report_agent` carrying state + `agent_session_id`, the only call the asset makes) for both `pane.get` and a receipt, the session-only path (`pane.report_agent_session`) for a receipt, a same-label pane holding a DIFFERENT session refused with `receiver_identity_mismatch` while the addressed session still receipts, and a REALLY DETECTED agent that never reported through a hook still `receiver_identity_unverified`. The M3-11 guards stay green unchanged: `hermes_session_claim_leaves_state_to_detection` (state ignored, session replacement) and the receipt characterization suite. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `920e1e1` row above)* | **A retired hook identity stays retired: the identity-only paths now apply the same retirement, freshness, sequencing and owner-coherence rules as the full-lifecycle path.** Codex Gate-2 M3 extension verdict (`msg_fd6336c8e9038990`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `920e1e1`, four P2 findings on the new `hook_identity`). The row above created `hook_identity` on two paths -- `record_identity_only_hook_report` and the `session_identity_only_integration` branch of `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` (`src/terminal/state.rs`) -- that skipped every guard the full-lifecycle funnel applies, so (1) a released Hermes session was resurrected by its own late callback and the SAME receipt flipped from `receiver_identity_unverified` back to `received`; (2) a detection observation captured BEFORE a newer identity report erased that identity and its persisted session; (3) `pane.clear_agent_authority` with `source = None` resolved its sequence fence from `hook_authority` only, so a stale clear erased a newer identity; and (4) an identity-only identity and a full-lifecycle authority could name different owners at once, after which the obsolete identity vetoed the accepted owner's `release` and that owner's own clear suppressed the live owner's later reports. **Fix -- one shared implementation, reached from both paths.** `hook_report_retirement_applies` names the owners retirement covers (full-lifecycle OR session-identity-only) and replaces the `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` gate inside the suppression/stale-session predicates, which are renamed off `full_lifecycle_*` accordingly (`hook_report_is_suppressed`, `hook_report_matches_stale_session`, `hook_report_has_fresh_session_after_suppression`, `hook_report_has_fresh_session_after_stale_session`, `suppress_hook_report`, `remember_stale_hook_session`, `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent`, and the `SuppressedHookReport`/`StaleHookSession`/`HookSuppressionReason` records they use). `hook_report_survives_retirement` is the single gate all three report paths call before `accept_hook_report`; `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting` is the single consumption step that turns a superseded suppression into a stale session; `owned_session_ref` resolves the session an owner holds in EITHER representation, so a release or clear suppresses the identity-only owner WITH the session it anchored; `retire_hook_identity` retires an identity the way the authority path retires `hook_authority` (suppress the owner, and on a process exit drop its sequence anchor). `HookIdentity` gains `reported_at`, `record_identity_only_hook_report_at` carries the report's own instant through the funnel, and the new `hook_identity_not_newer_than` guards the detection-side identity clear the way `hook_authority_not_newer_than` guards the authority clear; the persisted-session clear-on-exit is scoped by `persisted_session_is_anchored_by_hook_identity` so identity and session are retired coherently. `hook_report_is_stale` is the non-mutating half of `accept_hook_report`, letting `clear_hook_authority_with_mutation` fence a source-less clear against EVERY owner it would drop (authority and identity) before committing to any, and that clear now suppresses only the owner it actually clears. `release_agent_with_mutation` scopes the identity guard instead of vetoing: another owner's identity is preserved rather than dropped, but it no longer blocks the owner of the live authority from releasing, and an accepted full-lifecycle report retires an identity-only identity belonging to a different owner. **Not weakened.** Nothing trusts a detected label; the stored target session triple and the owner-coherence filter in `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs`) are untouched; restart is not banned -- a genuinely new session re-anchors after a release, and after a process exit a freshly observed process does, exactly as for a full-lifecycle owner. `src/app/actions.rs` and `src/app/creation.rs` needed no change: the dispatch still calls `record_identity_only_hook_report`, which now delegates to the `_at` variant. **Tests.** The reviewer's seven probes (`codex-m3-hook-identity-lifetime-920e1e1.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix: five `terminal::state` probes plus `tests/zynk_receipt.rs::retired_session_must_not_receipt_after_a_late_report` were RED on `920e1e1` and are green here, while the positive control `both_report_shapes_bind_the_addressed_session_only` (both reporter shapes, plus the same-label/wrong-session refusal) stays green in both states. Five more `terminal::state` tests pin the positive side: reacquiring after a release with a fresh session through each reporter shape, resuming after a process exit only on fresh process evidence, an identity-only clear leaving a different live owner's reports alone, and an obsolete identity not vetoing a coexisting owner's release. The named full-lifecycle counterparts stay green unchanged (`stale_process_exit_does_not_clear_newer_same_agent_hook_authority`, `stale_clear_all_sequence_is_checked_against_current_authority_source`) alongside the M3-11 Hermes guards. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `3b70f78` row above)* | **An explicit same-session resume is admitted again -- but only on process evidence gathered AFTER the retirement.** Codex Gate-2 M3 extension verdict (`msg_5fe4c9a3eff5f1a8`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `0af3637`, one remaining P2). The row above made `hook_report_survives_retirement` (`src/terminal/state.rs`) reject any report naming a session in `stale_hook_sessions`, and it ran BEFORE `session_start_source` was ever considered. After a release or an observed process exit, the next `None` -> agent detection transition moves the retired session out of `suppressed_hook_reports` into `stale_hook_sessions` (`clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent`), so a later `pane.report_agent_session` carrying `session_start_source = "resume"` and a higher sequence was refused **permanently** -- the identity and its session never came back, and with them the pane's receipt authority. That is the one shape a real resume must take: the Hermes resume command deliberately reuses `session_ref.value` (`src/agent_resume.rs`), so a NEW id would be a new session, not a resume. The existing positive `identity_resumes_after_process_exit_with_fresh_process_evidence` moves `hermes-1` -> `hermes-2`, so it proves RESTART, not resume, and left the gap uncovered. **Fix -- evidence, then shape, then commit.** `StaleHookSession` gains `fresh_process_evidence`. It is set true in exactly one place, `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent`, whose precondition IS the evidence (this owner was retired, and its agent is the detected process again) -- the same fresh-process notion the restart path already relies on; `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting` records false, because a superseded suppression proves nothing about a running process. Every new retirement clears the flag for that owner inside the single suppression funnel `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref`, so only an observation made after the LATEST retirement counts. `hook_report_survives_retirement` now takes `session_start_source` and splits the two rejections it used to fuse: a suppression still rejects unconditionally (a resume with nothing observed since the retirement is exactly the late callback the gate exists to refuse), while a stale-session match consults the new `explicit_session_start_reclaims_stale_session`, which admits the report only when BOTH hold -- it carries a session-start reason this owner actually starts on (`session_start_source_allows_session_replacement`, the same set the anchor-replacement clamp trusts; for `zynk:hermes` that is `startup`/`new`/`resume`), and the session it names is a stale session carrying fresh process evidence. A reclaim drops the sequence anchor the way a brand-new session does, but the stale entry itself survives the gate: `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting` (now also taking the agent label and the accepted session) removes it through the new `forget_stale_hook_session` only once the report has passed every remaining check, so a report this gate admits but a later guard refuses leaves the session retired, while an accepted reclaim stops being stale and this owner's ordinary follow-up reports for it are no longer refused. **Scope.** The two report shapes that carry no reason pass `None` and are unchanged: `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` (full lifecycle) and `record_identity_only_hook_report_at` (the shipped `pane.report_agent` shape) can never reclaim. Only `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` -- reached from `AppEvent::AgentSessionReported` (`src/app/actions.rs`) for `pane.report_agent_session` -- supplies one, so the rule lives in the shared gate and applies wherever a reason exists. **Not weakened.** The stale-session check is kept, not removed; nothing trusts a detected label (the reason comes from the hook report and the evidence from the retirement bookkeeping, never from `effective_agent_label`/`detected_agent`); the stored target session triple and the owner-coherence filter in `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs`) are untouched, and no app-side file changed. **Tests.** The reviewer's two probes (`codex-m3-same-session-resume-0af3637.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix -- `identity_same_session_resume_after_release_with_fresh_process_evidence` and `identity_same_session_resume_after_process_exit_with_fresh_process_evidence` -- were RED on `0af3637` and are green here, each also asserting the reclaimed session is no longer stale. Three fork-authored negatives pin the other half: `identity_same_session_resume_after_release_without_fresh_process_evidence_stays_retired` and its `..._after_process_exit_...` sibling (the explicit reason alone reclaims nothing), and `identity_late_callback_after_fresh_process_evidence_stays_retired`, which replays three non-start shapes against a freshly observed process -- the shipped `pane.report_agent` state callback, a reason-less `pane.report_agent_session`, and a `compact` reason `zynk:hermes` never starts on -- and requires all three to stay retired. At the API level `tests/zynk_receipt.rs::a_bare_same_session_resume_must_not_receipt_for_a_retired_session` sends the released owner's own `session_start_source = "resume"` for the session it just retired and requires the receipt to stay `receiver_identity_unverified`; it is green in both states, guarding against the naive fix. Every retirement negative, restart positive and reporter-shape control from the two rows above stays green unchanged, as do `stale_process_exit_does_not_clear_newer_same_agent_hook_authority` and `stale_clear_all_sequence_is_checked_against_current_authority_source`. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `ad7adba` row above)* | **Pending resume evidence expires when a NEWER observation shows that process gone -- whether or not an identity is installed.** Codex Gate-2 M3 extension verdict (`msg_f23d4dbab48a4c35`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `ad7adba`, one P2). The row above recorded `fresh_process_evidence` as a bare bool, cleared in exactly one place -- `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref` (`src/terminal/state.rs`), which only a RETIREMENT reaches. The detection path retires a hook identity only while one is installed, and between observing the replacement process and accepting its session report there is deliberately NONE: that is the whole window the reclaim exists to serve. So an exit observed inside it retired nothing and left the bit standing, and a delayed `pane.report_agent_session` carrying `session_start_source = "resume"` still reclaimed the retired session on evidence about a process that had since died -- restoring the identity and its session with `detected_agent` at `None`, which `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs`) then treats as the pane's receipt authority. **Fix -- evidence with a lifetime, not a latch.** `StaleHookSession::fresh_process_evidence` becomes `Option<Instant>`: not merely THAT the owner's process was seen again after the retirement but WHEN, which is what lets a later observation reason about it. `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent` takes the observation's `now`, stamps the sessions it newly stales, and re-arms the ones ALREADY stale for that agent -- so expiry voids a reclaim without banning the session, and the next genuinely fresh process admits the resume exactly as the first one did. `record_fresh_process_evidence` keeps the NEWEST of the two, so a reordered older observation cannot roll evidence back over a newer one. The new `expire_stale_session_evidence_on_process_loss` runs on EVERY detection observation in `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at`, deliberately OUTSIDE the identity-retirement block whose `hook_identity` precondition is the bug, and clears evidence strictly older than the observation on the same two limbs that retire an identity: this owner's process seen exiting (`process_exited` and the detected agent is the stale owner's), or a different agent detected in its place (mirroring `hook_identity_conflicts_with_detected_agent`, which likewise decides nothing when no agent is detected at all). Ordering is preserved the way `hook_identity_not_newer_than` preserves it for the identity itself: an observation captured BEFORE the evidence it would erase decides nothing. **Not weakened.** `explicit_session_start_reclaims_stale_session` still demands both limbs -- a session-start reason this owner starts on AND fresh evidence -- so every retirement negative is untouched; nothing here reads `effective_agent_label`/`detected_agent` for identity (the expiry consults the stale entry's own recorded label against the observation, never a detected label as authority); no app-side file changed. **Tests.** The reviewer's two probes (`codex-m3-resume-evidence-ad7adba.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix: `identity_same_session_resume_cannot_use_process_evidence_invalidated_by_a_later_exit` was RED on `ad7adba` and is green here, and its companion `identity_same_session_resume_keeps_process_evidence_after_an_older_exit_observation` -- green in both states -- pins the ordering half, so the fix cannot degenerate into "any exit clears it". One fork-authored positive closes the loop: `identity_same_session_resume_after_an_invalidating_exit_needs_new_process_evidence` requires the resume to be refused in the window after the invalidating exit and admitted again once the agent is observed running, proving expiry voids the reclaim rather than the session. Every positive, negative and reporter-shape control from the three rows above stays green unchanged, including both full-lifecycle stale-observation tests (`different_session_after_process_exit_waits_for_fresh_process_evidence`, `missing_session_after_process_exit_waits_for_fresh_process_evidence`) and `tests/zynk_receipt.rs::a_bare_same_session_resume_must_not_receipt_for_a_retired_session`. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `5ffafed` row above)* | **The latest process-loss observation is retained as a boundary of its own, so a reordered running observation cannot re-arm a retired session.** Codex Gate-2 M3 extension verdict (`msg_d2c0941e71e39abe`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `5ffafed`, one P2). The row above gave pending reclaim evidence a lifetime, but the ONLY timestamp a stale session kept was that evidence: `expire_stale_session_evidence_on_process_loss` (`src/terminal/state.rs`) set `fresh_process_evidence` back to `None`, and from that moment the session held no record of WHEN its process was last seen gone. Two consequences, both reachable through the production state methods. Every further loss observation decided nothing, because the loop read the evidence FIRST and skipped a session that had none; and `record_fresh_process_evidence` read `None` as permission to accept ANY observation, so the re-arm loop in `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent` restored evidence from a delayed running observation CAPTURED BEFORE the exit that had just voided it. "Keep the newest" therefore held only while the evidence was `Some`, never across an expiry: a Hermes session retired at t1, seen running again at t3 and seen exiting at t4 accepted a resume backed by a running observation captured at t3.5, although no process had run since the t4 exit; with a second exit at t8 -- observed while the evidence was already absent -- the same held for an observation captured at t6. **Fix -- the loss is a fact in its own right.** `StaleHookSession` gains `last_loss_observed_at: Option<Instant>` beside the evidence, and the two operations on that record move onto the record itself. `observe_process_loss` advances the boundary on every loss newer than it, whether or not there is any evidence left to expire, and expires the evidence only when the loss is strictly newer than it, so the older-exit control from the row above is unchanged. `record_fresh_process_evidence` becomes a method that refuses any observation not strictly newer than that boundary before applying the existing keep-the-newest rule, so BOTH re-arm paths -- the already-stale loop and the existing-entry branch of `remember_stale_hook_session` -- are gated by one implementation rather than by the absence of evidence. `expire_stale_session_evidence_on_process_loss` keeps the same two limbs and the same call site on every detection observation; it now evaluates the loss predicate first and delegates the record-keeping. **Not weakened.** The boundary bans nothing permanently: a running observation genuinely newer than the latest loss re-arms the session and the explicit same-session resume is admitted exactly as before, which the ported probes assert directly. `explicit_session_start_reclaims_stale_session` still demands both limbs -- a session-start reason this owner starts on AND fresh evidence; nothing reads `effective_agent_label`/`detected_agent` for identity (the loss predicate consults the stale entry's own recorded label against the observation); the retirement funnel `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref` still scopes evidence to the LATEST retirement; no app-side file changed. **Tests.** The reviewer's two probes (`codex-m3-resume-observation-order-5ffafed.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix: `reordered_running_observation_cannot_undo_a_later_exit` and `reordered_running_observation_cannot_undo_a_second_exit` were both RED on `5ffafed` ("a running observation older than the latest exit re-armed the retired session") and are green here. Each also requires a genuinely newer running observation to re-admit the same-session resume, so the boundary cannot degenerate into a permanent ban, and the second drives its stale observation past a loss observed while the evidence was already absent -- the half a boundary tied to the evidence cannot express. Every positive, negative and reporter-shape control from the four rows above stays green unchanged, including `identity_same_session_resume_keeps_process_evidence_after_an_older_exit_observation`, `identity_same_session_resume_cannot_use_process_evidence_invalidated_by_a_later_exit`, `identity_same_session_resume_after_an_invalidating_exit_needs_new_process_evidence`, both same-session resume positives, all three retirement negatives, `identity_late_callback_after_fresh_process_evidence_stays_retired`, both full-lifecycle stale-session generation tests (`stale_session_suppression_survives_multiple_release_generations`, `stale_session_suppression_survives_multiple_process_generations`) and `tests/zynk_receipt.rs::a_bare_same_session_resume_must_not_receipt_for_a_retired_session`. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| test-only follow-up: deterministic fake-hermes start | `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` | **The identity-only receipt tests now start their fake `hermes` the way a real agent starts -- as a forked child in its OWN foreground process group -- behind a ready marker with bounded resends.** **Corrected cause.** The `test-only follow-up to 920e1e1` row under the M4 heading below blamed detection latency and raised the deadline from 10 s to 30 s; that attribution was wrong and the raised deadline changed nothing (4 of 20 full parallel `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` runs still failed here, each burning the whole 30 s). `should_probe_foreground_job` (`src/pane.rs`) re-probes a pane that has no agent yet ONLY when the foreground PROCESS GROUP changes, or while the content-driven acquisition window (`PROCESS_ACQUISITION_WINDOW`, 8 s from the pane's first output, rechecked every 500 ms then every 2 s) is still open. The old command `exec bash -c 'exec -a hermes cat'` replaced the pane shell IN PLACE, so the process group never changed, and the silent `cat` then emitted nothing that could reopen the window (`sync_content_change_acquisition` restarts it only on new content). When the injected command landed after the window's last recheck, the pane was never probed again and detection could never report `hermes` -- permanently stuck, not slow, so no deadline could have fixed it. Captured at the deadline: `ps` showed the `hermes`-named `cat` alive as its own session leader AND the tty's foreground process group (`Ss+`, `tpgid == pgid`) while `pane.get` still carried no `agent` at all. **Fix (test-only).** Drop the leading `exec` so the fake agent is a forked child with its own foreground process group, which forces the probe on the next tick regardless of the acquisition window -- and which is also the shape a real agent typed at a prompt has. Keep the `bash -c 'exec -a hermes cat'` layer (argv[0] = `hermes` is what `identify_agent_in_job` reads) without assuming the pane's own shell implements `exec -a`. **Ready marker.** A new shared helper `run_in_pane_until_ready` sends the command, waits up to 10 s for a quote-split `printf` marker emitted just before the agent starts, and resends up to 3 times (each resend led by a newline so a half-delivered line cannot be glued to the front); `start_passive_cat` was refactored onto it and gains the retry. The marker covers the other, real race in this class -- a shell still initializing its line editor discards queued tty input, so a `pane run` can vanish with an `ok` result -- and, by splitting the two stages, it is what proved the command had ALWAYS run and moved the investigation onto the probe gate. Both stages now fail with their own message naming which one gave up. The 30 s detection deadline is kept as headroom. **Evidence.** Before: 4/20 parallel suite runs failed (33-35 s each). After: 25/25 runs at 44/44 (~3 s each, no outliers), 12 solo runs each of `a_same_label_identity_only_pane_with_another_session_cannot_receipt` and `both_report_shapes_bind_the_addressed_session_only`, full suite 2931/2931, `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean. **No production change** -- the probe-gate behaviour above is left exactly as it is; an agent that `exec`s in place over the pane shell after the acquisition window has closed is not currently re-probed, which is recorded here as an observation, not a fix. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |

# LINUX-ONLY + v0.8.2 PORT LEDGER — M4 integrations + asset parity

The upstream `v0.7.1..v0.8.2` integration, hook-asset and asset-version-parity port, on branch
`port/linux-only-v0.8.2`. The licensing split of the M2 section above still applies: an upstream commit
dated before `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22) arrives under AGPL-3.0-or-later, a later one under Apache-2.0, and
only the latter appends its touched files to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list.

| zynk | upstream | what / where / why / adaptations |
|---|---|---|
| *(this commit)* | `12cce7de` *(pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **The single 6206-line `src/integration/mod.rs` is split into upstream's ten-file layout as a pure move; no behaviour changes.** New `src/integration/{types,version,command,file_ops,env,registry,config_edit,targets,actions,tests}.rs`; `mod.rs` keeps only the 57 consts plus the 9 `mod` declarations, the 5 `pub(crate) use` re-export groups and `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;`. The fork's OWN bodies were moved into upstream's file layout — upstream's bodies were never imported — and each destination preserves the fork's relative item order, which reproduces upstream's order exactly once the twelve items the fork does not have are removed. Import style matches upstream per file so the 29 later `src/integration/**` commits keep their anchors: `registry.rs` uses `use super::env::*;` plus inline `super::CONST`/`super::Type`, everything else uses explicit `use super::{…}` lists. **Fork-only placements** (no upstream counterpart): `ZYNK_{PANE,TAB,WORKSPACE}_ID_ENV_VAR` → `env.rs` (re-exported from `mod.rs`; consumed by `src/pane.rs`), `INTEGRATION_ID_MARKER` stays in `mod.rs` beside its sibling `INTEGRATION_VERSION_MARKER` and is reached as `super::INTEGRATION_ID_MARKER`, and the three native-hook identity predicates `hook_has_herdr_residue` / `hook_is_native` / `expected_integration_id` → `registry.rs` (they are read only by `integration_status_at`). **Fork-only imports**: `env.rs` adds `use crate::config_dir::{config_dir_from_env_or_home, expand_tilde_path, home_dir};` (those three helpers left this module in the v0.7.0 port) and `tests.rs` adds `use portable_pty::CommandBuilder;` (upstream's `tests.rs` has no such test; the fork's `apply_pane_base_env_exports_zynk_socket_path` does). **NOT placed** — twelve upstream items the fork does not have, and no slot is reserved for them: the three `HERDR_*_ENV_VAR` consts (renamed), `config_dir_from_env_or_home` / `expand_tilde_path` / `home_dir` (now `crate::config_dir`), and the Linux-only-M1 casualties `integration_target_supported`, `legacy_bash_hook_path`, both `remove_legacy_bash_hook_file` arms, `windows_command_quote` and the Windows `qodercli_command_names`; consequently `config_edit.rs` drops upstream's `#[cfg(windows)] use super::file_ops::legacy_bash_hook_path;`, `targets.rs` drops `remove_legacy_bash_hook_file` from its `super::file_ops::{…}` list, and `actions.rs` drops `integration_target_supported` from its `super::registry::{…}` list. No `#[cfg(windows)]`, `#[cfg(unix)]`, `.ps1` or PowerShell branch entered the tree; the two `"powershell"` JSON **keys** in `config_edit.rs` are unchanged data-compat keys, not target branches. **Mechanical edits, three in total**: `AgentVersionRequirement`'s four fields gain `pub` (cross-module field access); `enforce_agent_version`'s two `{INSTALL_WARNING_PREFIX}` inline format captures become positional `{}` with a `super::INSTALL_WARNING_PREFIX` argument, because a path cannot be inline-captured; and rustfmt reflows a closure in `integration_specs` into a block once `pub(crate) ` widens the line. **One deviation from upstream**: `registry.rs::integration_specs` is `pub(crate)` here, where upstream deliberately leaves it private — the fork-only test `all_native_status_assets_pass_identity_gate` calls `integration_specs().len()` from `tests.rs`, which after the split is a sibling module; verified by rebuilding with it private (`error[E0425]: cannot find function integration_specs in this scope`). Every other item follows upstream's rule of `private → pub(crate)` except `actions.rs::install_target_inner`, which stays private. **Loss proof**: 321 items before and after with an empty symmetric difference; the 104 `tests` fn names and the 97 `#[test]` attributes are identical sets; the token multiset has an empty `only-in-old`; and a reflow-immune per-item body comparison reports exactly the three mechanical edits above. `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` reports **97 tests run, 97 passed, 2505 skipped** both before and after, and the full suite is 2908/2908. |
| test-only follow-up to 920e1e1 (`start_detected_hermes`) | `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` | The identity-only receipt tests wait for real foreground detection of a fake `hermes` process; the 10 s deadline expired in 2 of 6 full parallel runs of the receipt+CLI suite on 2026-09-08 (`the hermes process was never detected`) while the test passed alone and on rerun — detection latency under parallel server spawning, not a product defect. Deadline raised to 30 s with a comment; no production change. |

The fork-only `pi_integration_version_marker_matches_const` stays, as the narrower guard. **Baseline pinned by this commit** (asset marker == const, verified for every one): pi 5, omp 3, claude 7, codex 6, kimi 4, copilot 2, devin 2, droid 2, opencode 7, kilo 2, hermes 3, qodercli 2, cursor 1 — twelve at upstream `v0.7.1` parity, pi at +2 from the fork's own state-only pi rewrite. **Red proof:** drifting `src/integration/assets/droid/zynk-agent-state.sh` to `=3` fails the new test (`droid asset version must match its integration version constant`, left `Some(3)` right `Some(2)`) while `pi_integration_version_marker_matches_const` still passes; the drift was reverted. `cargo nextest run --bin zynk integration::` 99/99, up from 97. |

the only prose rename inside the new code is the upstream product name in the release-policy comment of `shouldReleaseOnSessionShutdown` -> "zynk". Switching `effective_state_change` from `None` to `recompute_effective_state(...)` as upstream does is inert for identity-only reports, because `effective_agent_label` and `effective_known_agent` read `hook_authority` and `detected_agent` only, never `hook_identity`. **`Agent::Omp` does not exist in this fork** (the enum slot upstream gives `Omp` holds `Mastracode`, and `lookup_agent` has no `omp` alias), so omp is a hook-only identity and `parse_agent_label("omp")` is `None`. Upstream's adopted test opens with `set_detected_state(Some(Agent::Omp), AgentState::Idle)` purely to clear the known-agent conflict gate; that line is dropped with a comment, since an unparseable label clears the same gate. **New Bun harness:** upstream's shared asset test alongside the two extension directories arrives as `src/integration/assets/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` with `ZYNK_ENV` / `ZYNK_PANE_ID` / `ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`, module paths `./pi/zynk-agent-state.ts` and `./omp/zynk-agent-state.ts`, and socket temp prefixes `zynk-…` / `zynk-pi-retry-…`; it carries the `importFresh` cache-busting import and the drop-first-response server from `b1d1a07b`. Upstream's new `integration-assets-test` recipe is **not** adopted: the fork's `justfile` already has `test-ts`, wired into `ci` and therefore `check`, so the new file is added as a second line of that recipe (port sheet §2.06 recommendation). **The fork's own pi Bun suite needed two real adaptations, not cosmetic ones:** `agent_start` now calls `updateSessionRef(ctx)`, so a fake that emits it with no context would wipe the session ref captured at `session_start` — the suite gains a shared `rootSessionContext()` and a `startAgent()` helper that pass the same context the pi runtime does; and `session_shutdown` now releases only on a quit, so the release assertion emits `{ reason: "quit" }`. A new fork test asserts the other half, that `{ reason: "reload" }` emits no `pane.release_agent`. **Version pins updated in the same commit:** `pi_asset_is_state_only_no_receiver` 5 -> 6, the Bun marker pin `// ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=5` -> `=6` with its test name, and `tests/cli_wrapper.rs` `pi: current (v5)` -> `(v6)`. **Tests adopted:** ten upstream asset-substring tests plus upstream's `omp_handler` slicing helper (four from `3c7327c2`, six from `c5c30759`), and the state test `omp_resume_session_report_reanchors_full_lifecycle_authority`. Upstream replaces its `omp_session_hook_ignores_non_ui_sessions` with `omp_root_activation_requires_ui_context`; the fork never had the former and keeps its own `omp_root_session_guard_is_instance_scoped`, which still passes because omp retains `ctx?.hasUI !== true` inside `activateRootSession`. **Two fork-authored Rust guards** cover the behaviour upstream only tests under Bun: `pi_extension_retries_an_unanswered_state_report` (the `b1d1a07b` attempt/retry pair) and `pi_and_omp_extensions_restore_working_state_on_reload` (the `d5521e3d` line, both assets). **Excluded:** `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md`, upstream's `integration-assets-test` recipe name and its `plugin-marketplace-test` wiring. `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` **111 passed**, up from 99; `terminal::` 249 passed; `agent_resume::` 47 passed; the full suite is 2946/2946 with 2 skipped; `just test-ts` is 3 passed in the new shared harness and 10 in the pi suite; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean. |
