| *(this commit)* | `be901fea` `60c04005` `f23e3779` *(all pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **opencode's `session.status` is read in its object form, a subagent's permission/question prompt projects `blocked` onto the pane without stealing the root session, and every lifecycle report is serialized behind one request chain; `OPENCODE_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 7 -> 9.** Three upstream commits land together because all three edit the same plugin asset and the third refactors the mapping the first two introduce. **`be901fea` (7 -> 8):** `stateFromSessionStatus` accepted only a bare string, so opencode's newer `{ type: "idle" | "busy" | "retry" }` payload fell through to `reportSession(sessionID)` and the pane never left its previous state; the reader now takes `typeof status === "string" ? status : status?.type` and `retry` joins the working set. Its second half fixes a real blind spot: child (task-tool) session events were dropped wholesale, so a subagent waiting on a permission prompt left the pane reading `working` forever. `permission.asked`/`question.asked` on a child now report `blocked` and `permission.replied`/`question.replied`/`question.rejected` report `working` — **state only, with no `agent_session_id`**, so the pane keeps the root session id it already holds. **`60c04005` (8 -> 9):** `request()` becomes a thin wrapper that chains onto `requestChain` and delegates to the extracted `requestOnce()`, so two reports can no longer race two sockets and land out of order; `reportState` records the session it reported under in `reportedRootSessionID`, and `session.updated` now re-reports only when the id actually changed, which stops the redundant `pane.report_agent_session` that followed every state report. **`f23e3779` (no bump):** pure refactor — the child-event switch becomes the `CHILD_EVENT_STATES` map and the status switch becomes `SESSION_STATE_BY_STATUS`; behaviour is unchanged and verified by the two tests it adds. **Adaptations:** standing renames only (`zynk-agent-state.js`, `ZynkAgentStatePlugin`, `zynk:opencode`, `ZYNK_*` env, `// ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=9`). The fork's pre-existing double-fallback rebrand artifact in this asset (`process.env.ZYNK_PANE_ID ?? process.env.ZYNK_PANE_ID`, and the parenthesized `(process.env.ZYNK_ENV ?? process.env.ZYNK_ENV) !== "1"` gate the fork added because `??` binds looser than `!==`) is **carried through unchanged** — it is load-bearing for the sibling pi asset assertions at `src/integration/assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts:83-88`, and unlike the new mastracode asset this is an existing file, not a fresh one. The rename map was proved complete before any edit: upstream `v0.7.1`'s asset passed through it diffs against the fork's file in exactly the three gate lines above and nowhere else. **EXCLUDED — the windows hunk that sits between these commits.** Upstream's asset at `f23e3779` already carries `a51a1591`'s `const socketEndpoint = process.platform === "win32" ? ...` named-pipe branch and its `net.createConnection(socketEndpoint, ...)` call site. `a51a1591` is M4-07's commit and its windows branches are a standing exclude for this linux-only fork, so the target file was reconstructed as upstream at `60c04005` with `f23e3779`'s asset patch applied on top (clean, one -3 line offset) rather than copied from `f23e3779` — the branch never entered the tree and `socketPath` is still passed straight to `net.createConnection`. **New Bun suite:** `src/integration/assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.test.ts`, 193 lines (142 from `60c04005` + 51 from `f23e3779`), four tests. It mocks `node:net` rather than binding a real socket like the fork's shared `assets/zynk-agent-state.test.ts`, which is what lets it hold a response open and prove ordering: `serializes lifecycle reports` (a second report opens no second connection until the first is acknowledged, and `seq` increments by exactly one), `suppresses redundant same-session updates`, `reports retry status as working`, `reports child prompts without replacing the root session` (five reports, every `agent_session_id` `undefined`). Wired as the third line of the fork's `test-ts` recipe in `justfile` (the fork's name for upstream's `integration-assets-test`), so it runs under `ci` and `check`. **A corrected port-sheet claim.** The M4 sheet §6 and the M4-05 brief both require the OPENCODE row of `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` to move 7 -> 9 in this commit. No such edit exists to make: the row adopted in M4-02 is `("opencode", OPENCODE_PLUGIN_ASSET, OPENCODE_INTEGRATION_VERSION)` — it reads the const rather than a literal, so bumping the const is the whole change and the test enforces the marker against it automatically. `src/integration/tests.rs` is therefore untouched by this commit, and no hardcoded opencode version literal exists anywhere in `src/` or `tests/`. **Red proofs, so no assertion is vacuous:** dropping the `requestChain` wrapper fails `serializes lifecycle reports`; dropping the `sessionID !== reportedRootSessionID` guard fails `suppresses redundant same-session updates`; removing the `["retry", "working"]` row fails `reports retry status as working`; reverting `stateFromSessionStatus` to string-only fails three of the four; deleting the `CHILD_EVENT_STATES` lookup fails `reports child prompts without replacing the root session`; and holding the const at 8 with the marker at 9 fails `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` (`opencode asset version must match its integration version constant`, left `Some(9)` right `Some(8)`). All six reverted and green, the asset byte-identical to its pre-mutation state. **Runtime proof beyond the unit tests:** against an isolated `HOME`, `zynk integration install opencode` wrote the plugin byte-identically to the embedded asset and `zynk integration status` reported `opencode: current (v9)`; driving that installed file against a real Unix socket with `ZYNK_ENV=1` produced exactly six requests in order — `{type:"retry"}` and the legacy bare string `"busy"` both `working` with `agent_session_id: root`, a same-id `session.updated` suppressed entirely, a child `permission.asked` `blocked` and a child `question.replied` `working` **both with no `agent_session_id`**, `{type:"idle"}` `idle` with `agent_session_id: root`, and a different-id `session.updated` as `pane.report_agent_session` for `other` — with `seq` strictly increasing across all six, and the child `session.created` itself producing no request. **Identity invariants:** the asset reports under the `zynk:opencode` source label, which `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` (`src/detect/mod.rs:319`) already pairs with `opencode`; nothing here reads a detected agent, and the child-prompt path deliberately omits `agent_session_id` precisely so the stored session triple and the owner-coherence check keep the root session. All three upstream commits predate `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), so they arrive under AGPL-3.0-or-later and **no** `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* entry is added. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=~/workspace/.zynk-dev-target` + isolated `HOME`/`ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`, short `TMPDIR` for the SUN_LEN budget noted in the row above):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` 116/116 (unchanged — this commit adds no Rust test); the full `--bin zynk` suite 2644/2644 passed, 1 skipped; `just test-ts` 17/17 across three files (3 shared + 10 pi + 4 new opencode, up from 13). |
| *(this commit)* | `adbaae68` *(non-test hunks)* `9e4fdd61` *(both pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **A pi session replacement re-anchors the pane's status on the new session instead of holding the one it replaced, and omp retries a lifecycle report the socket accepted but never answered.** `adbaae68`: the pi extension threads the session-start reason onto the wire — `reportSession(sessionStartSource?)` adds `session_start_source` to `pane.report_agent_session`, and `session_start` becomes `async (event, ctx)` so it `await`s that report before `publishState(true)`; state published ahead of the acknowledgement would still be attributed to the replaced session. On the Rust side `session_start_source_allows_session_replacement` gains `("zynk:pi", "pi", Some("new" | "resume" | "fork"))` at upstream's position (after the opencode arm, before omp's), and `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` FORGETS the stale entry for the session it is about to anchor whenever the start source is one this owner may replace on. `9e4fdd61`: omp mirrors pi's retry — `sendRequestNow` splits into `sendRequestAttempt(request, timeoutMs) -> Promise<boolean>` plus an `async` wrapper that retries once at 1500 ms when the 500 ms attempt goes unanswered, so a dropped first response no longer loses the report to the serialized queue. **Bumps:** `PI_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 6 -> 7 and `OMP_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 4 -> 5, each with its asset marker in this commit (upstream moves pi 4 -> 5 and omp 4 -> 5; the fork's pi sits +2 from its own state-only rewrite). **Already present, taken as no delta:** upstream's new `forget_stale_full_lifecycle_hook_session` helper — this fork generalized the map to `stale_hook_sessions` (both hook shapes) and already carries the identical helper as `forget_stale_hook_session`, reached from `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting`; only the CALL SITE inside `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` was missing, and that is the genuine delta. `adbaae68`'s two `src/integration/tests.rs` additions (`bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions`, `outdated_integrations_detect_previous_pi_version`) landed in M4-02 and are not re-taken here. **Adaptation:** upstream gates the forget on `session_replacement_allowed || foreground_takeover_allowed`; this fork has no takeover path (`foreground_agent_confirms_different_owner_takeover` and `suppress_current_full_lifecycle_hook_authority` do not exist — `current_session_owner_conflicts` simply refuses), so the gate is `session_replacement_allowed` alone. **Identity invariants held (review of every new `state.rs` path):** the new replacement arm is a pure function of `(source, agent_label, session_start_source)` — all three come off the hook report, none from `detected_agent`; `zynk:pi`/`pi` is in `crate::detect::full_lifecycle_hook_authority`, so the arm cannot widen any session-identity-only owner. The new forget call reads only `source`/`agent_label`/`session_ref` from the report and mutates `stale_hook_sessions`; it never touches `hook_authority`, `hook_identity` or `detected_agent`, and it sits after the identity-only retirement gate, after the `replacing_identity_only_session && !process_present` check and after both owner-conflict gates — upstream's exact position. It is provably INERT for a session-identity-only owner: that branch already ends with `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting(.., Some(&session_ref))`, whose last act is the same `forget_stale_hook_session(source, agent_label, accepted_ref)`, so forget -> remember_stale(suppressed) -> forget leaves the same final state as remember_stale(suppressed) -> forget. No identity-only branch is touched. The four adopted tests assert on `terminal.hook_authority` only; their `set_detected_state(Some(Agent::Pi), AgentState::Idle)` exists solely to clear `known_agent_label_conflicts_with_detected_agent`, exactly as upstream, and never sources identity. **Red proof (both hunks, reverted then restored):** dropping the forget call fails `pi_resume_reactivates_a_previously_stale_session` at `assert!(working.is_some())` — a resumed session stays retired and every later state report for it is refused; dropping the pi arm fails `pi_session_replacement_reports_reanchor_full_lifecycle_authority` for all three reasons. Each was restored and `src/terminal/state.rs` re-verified byte-identical. **Asset proof:** normalizing upstream `adbaae68:assets/pi/…` and `9e4fdd61:assets/omp/…` through the standing rename set reproduces the fork assets exactly, leaving only the four known fork divergences — the duplicated `process.env.ZYNK_* ?? process.env.ZYNK_*` reads behind `zynkEnv`, the version marker offset, omp's fork-local placement of `parseDurationEnv` after `reportSession`, and the upstream product name in the `shouldReleaseOnSessionShutdown` release-policy comment. The body becomes a single-quoted `python3 -c` script that reads `json.load(sys.stdin)` (kimi takes its action from `sys.argv[1]`, passed as the trailing `"$action"`), builds the request in one `json.dumps`, and — for kimi and cursor — sends it through a `with socket.socket(...) as client` block; the id is now the deterministic `f"zynk:<agent>:{seq}"` instead of `time`+`random.randrange`. That argv was verified against the fork's own dispatcher (`src/cli/pane.rs:41`): the real binary accepts it and proceeds to the socket connect, while an unknown flag exits 2 with `unknown option:`. | *(this commit)* | `7e821ba7` + `5f1957c2` *(ASSET half; both POST-relicense — `NOTICE` §4(b) entries below)* | **omp's extension installer stops borrowing pi's config directory, and the bundled hermes plugin stops reporting lifecycle state so it reports only the session identity the fork already treats it as authoritative for.** **`7e821ba7` (omp config dir).** `omp_extension_dir()` (`src/integration/env.rs`) resolved through `config_dir_from_env_or_home(PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR_ENV_VAR, [".omp", "agent"])`, so setting `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` -- pi's own relocation variable -- pointed omp at pi's extensions directory and the two agents overwrote each other's extension. It now resolves in explicit priority order: `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` (tilde-expanded, used verbatim as the agent dir) -> `$PI_CONFIG_DIR/agent/extensions` under `$HOME` -> `~/.omp/agent/extensions`. The new const `OMP_CONFIG_DIR_ENV_VAR = "PI_CONFIG_DIR"` is deliberately NOT renamed to a `ZYNK_*` name and carries a comment saying why: it is the omp agent's OWN variable, read by omp to find its config, not part of the `ZYNK_*` env surface (ADR 0010 §7 governs zynk's own names, not third-party ones), so the extension has to land where omp will look. `install_omp()` (`src/integration/targets.rs`) additionally hard-errors before creating or writing anything when `omp_extension_dir() == pi_extension_dir()`, which is exactly the shape `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` produces for both agents; the guard runs ahead of `ensure_extension_dir` and `remove_legacy_pi_extension_from_omp_dir`, so a shared directory can no longer lose its pi extension to omp's legacy-cleanup pass. **Tests** (`src/integration/tests.rs`): `clear_integration_path_env` clears the new variable; `install_omp_uses_pi_coding_agent_dir_env` is renamed to `install_omp_uses_pi_config_dir_env` and rewritten onto `HOME` + `PI_CONFIG_DIR=custom-omp` (its old body now names the refused shared-directory case, which is the point of the fix); and the new `install_omp_refuses_shared_pi_extension_directory` sets both variables, asserts the error text and the resolved path, and asserts the pi extension survived while no omp extension was written. `integration::` goes 121 -> 122. **`5f1957c2` ASSET half (deferred here from M3-11).** M3-11 (`2be143c`) took the detect half -- hermes left `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` for `session_identity_only_integration`, so its hook reports no longer set lifecycle state and the screen manifest carries it instead -- but left the shipped plugin still emitting nine lifecycle hooks, whose `state` the fork now discards. `src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py` is brought to upstream's post-commit shape: the docstring becomes "report resumable session identity"; `_INTERACTIVE_PLATFORMS = {cli, tui, desktop, acp}` is added; `_session_id`, `_report`, `_working`, `_blocked` and `_idle` are deleted; and `_report_session(start_source, **kwargs)` (platform-gated, non-empty-`str` session id, emitting `pane.report_agent_session` with `agent_session_id` + `session_start_source`) plus `_session_started` (`startup`), `_session_reset` (`new`) and `_session_observed` (`resume`, `platform == "cli"` only) replace them. `register()` drops from nine hooks to exactly `{on_session_start, on_session_reset, pre_llm_call}`. Net effect: a subagent or background turn can no longer repoint the pane's identity, and a resumed CLI session recovers its id on the first turn instead of never. `_SOURCE = "zynk:hermes"`, `_AGENT`, `_base_params` and `_send` are untouched, and the marker stays `# ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=3` against `HERMES_INTEGRATION_VERSION` -- **no bump in this commit** (upstream's `4` here comes from a `_session_started` arm the fork's asset never had; `50ddc06f` takes the fork to `4` in M4-13, which must land after this row because it rewrites `_send` into `_send_session`). **The four obsolete assertions** in `bundled_integration_assets_report_session_refs` (`src/integration/tests.rs`) are deleted: they pinned `session_id = _session_id(kwargs)` and `pane.report_agent",`, both of which the asset no longer contains. Upstream deleted eight; the fork only ever had four, because the fork's asset never carried upstream's `session_start_source: startup` / `on_session_end` lines. The other six `HERMES_PLUGIN_INIT_ASSET` uses stay: the registry row, the install round-trip, the native-identity gate `all_native_status_assets_pass_identity_gate`, and the four `uninstall_hermes_*` fixtures. **New `scripts/test_hermes_integration_asset.py`** replaces them, loading the asset by path under the module id `zynk_hermes_integration` (upstream's `herdr_` prefix rebranded), stubbing `_send` and driving `register()` against a fake context: `test_reports_only_root_session_identity` pins the hook set to exactly those three and asserts that five hook invocations produce exactly two `pane.report_agent_session` calls (the subagent-platform and background-`pre_llm_call` ones are dropped), and `test_first_turn_recovers_resumed_session_identity` pins the CLI resume path. It is wired into BOTH `python3 -m unittest` lists in the `justfile` (`test` and `check`), taking the maintenance suite to ten modules, and `CLAUDE.md`'s enumeration of that list is updated to match. **`tests/zynk_receipt.rs`** keeps its five identity-only receipt tests unchanged and green (25/25, run twice): they build their reports as raw socket JSON rather than by executing the asset, and `pane.report_agent` carrying `state` + `agent_session_id` remains a live wire shape every other integration sends and a pre-`5f1957c2` installed hermes plugin keeps sending, so the identity-only routing must still accept it. antigravity-cli is SESSION-IDENTITY-ONLY — `session_identity_only_integration` (`src/detect/mod.rs`) gains `("zynk:antigravity_cli", "agy")` and it is NEVER added to `full_lifecycle_hook_authority`, so its `pane.report_agent_session` lands on `hook_identity` (the M3 identity-fix path) and its lifecycle stays screen-detected. grok is added to `is_reserved_native_state_source` instead, matching upstream, which never puts grok in either detect gate. Both gain `is_official_agent_source` + resume arms (`agy --conversation <id>`, `grok --resume <id>`); a `Path` session ref returns `None` for both. Note the label asymmetry: source `zynk:antigravity_cli`, agent label `agy`. **Adaptations:** hook config file `herdr.json` → `zynk.json`; `ANTIGRAVITY_CLI_HOOK_BLOCK_NAME` `"herdr"` → `"zynk"`; the `#[cfg(windows)]`/`#[cfg(not(windows))]` `ANTIGRAVITY_CLI_HOOK_INSTALL_NAME`/`_HOOK_ASSET` pairs collapse to one `include_str!` and the `.ps1` twin is dropped (linux-only fork, M1); the `integration_target_supported` hunk is skipped (that gate does not exist in this fork). **Deliberate divergences:** both `IntegrationTarget::ALL` hunks are SKIPPED — the fork has no `ALL` until M8, where it is sized once; and `session_start_source_allows_session_replacement` keeps its fork name (upstream renames it to `session_report_allows_session_replacement`) and gains only the `("zynk:antigravity_cli", "agy", None)` arm, so `src/terminal/state.rs` stays a two-hunk additive diff while the B1 identity work merges. Upstream's table rewrite of `hermes_session_claim_leaves_state_to_detection` is likewise not taken; the hermes characterization is left byte-identical and antigravity-cli gets its own twin test `antigravity_cli_session_claim_leaves_state_to_detection`. **Tests:** `integration_target_wire_ids_are_stable` gains the `antigravity_cli` and `grok` wire ids; `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions`, `all_native_status_assets_pass_identity_gate` and `bundled_integration_assets_report_session_refs` gain both assets; 13 new install / uninstall / status tests in `src/integration/tests.rs` (122 → 135 under `integration::`), including grok's seven nonfunctional-config shapes; `session_identity_integrations_leave_state_to_screen_detection` (renamed by upstream from the hermes-only test) covers both members; and `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` gains `antigravity_cli_session_report_anchors_its_receipt` + `a_detection_only_antigravity_cli_pane_is_not_receipt_capable`, with `start_detected_hermes` generalized to `start_detected_agent(fixture, pane, agent)` (fork the fake agent as a CHILD behind a quote-split ready marker) and re-exposed as `start_detected_hermes` / `start_detected_agy`. **Bumps:** new `GROK_INTEGRATION_VERSION = 1` and `ANTIGRAVITY_CLI_INTEGRATION_VERSION = 1`, each paired with its asset marker in this commit. |
| *(this commit)* | `1491b7dd` `a07871a5` `5eab32da` *(all **post-relicense** — `NOTICE` updated)* | **A codex sub-session nested inside another codex session can no longer steal the pane's identity, omp accepts a Windows-shaped session path, and pi activates only on a real TUI session.** `1491b7dd`: the codex hook asset gains two payload-keyed guards — it exits unless the `SessionStart` payload carries a non-blank `transcript_path` (an ephemeral side session persists none), and it exits when the inherited `CODEX_THREAD_ID` env var names a session other than the one in the payload. Identity therefore stays hook-payload-authoritative: both guards read the hook input and the process env, never detection. `src/pane.rs` closes the other half — `apply_pane_launch_env` now `env_remove`s `CODEX_THREAD_ID` (named by the new `CODEX_THREAD_ID_ENV_VAR` const beside `PaneLaunchEnv`) so a pane spawned from inside a codex session does not hand the outer thread id to the codex it launches. `a07871a5`: upstream's Windows fix lives entirely inside the **TypeScript** omp asset, so it ships on Linux too — `updateSessionRef` stops testing `file.startsWith("/")` and calls the new exported `isAbsoluteSessionPath()` (`path.posix.isAbsolute || path.win32.isAbsolute`, `import path from "node:path"`). Dropping it would leave the omp asset a revision behind and break the marker/const parity chain. `5eab32da`: pi's `session_start` gate moves from `ctx?.hasUI !== true` to `ctx?.mode !== "tui"` — RPC mode also reports `hasUI: true`, so mode is the reliable gate for "there is a PTY zynk can display". **omp keeps `ctx?.hasUI !== true`** (`omp_root_activation_requires_ui_context`, `omp_root_session_guard_is_instance_scoped`); the guard was deliberately not swept across both assets. Bumps, const + `# ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=` marker in this commit: CODEX `6 -> 7`, OMP `6 -> 7`, PI `8 -> 9` (fork pi runs +1 on upstream 8 from its own state-only rewrite). Hardcoded pins moved with them: `codex_v2_integration_status_is_outdated` `expected_version` `6 -> 7`, `pi_asset_is_state_only_no_receiver` `Some(8) -> Some(9)`, the Bun marker pin in the fork-authored `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` (`=8 -> =9`, test renamed), and `tests/cli_wrapper.rs` `pi: current (v8) -> (v9)`. Tests: `bundled_integration_assets_report_session_refs` gains `CODEX_HOOK_ASSET.contains("CODEX_THREAD_ID")` plus a pi-scoped `ctx?.mode !== "tui"` pair — the fork never carried upstream's `PI_EXTENSION_ASSET.contains("ctx?.hasUI !== true")` line, so `5eab32da`'s one-line swap is an **addition** here (positive on the new gate, negative on the old one), which keeps the omp-vs-pi regression trap explicit. The shared Bun harness `assets/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` gains `mode: "tui"` on the five pi-driving contexts and the two new upstream tests *OMP accepts POSIX and Windows session paths* and *Pi ignores RPC sessions even when UI APIs are available*; the fork's `rootSessionContext()` in `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` gains the same `mode: "tui"`. **Upstream's `tests/cli/hooks.rs` and `tests/cli/sessions.rs` do not exist in the fork yet** (the `tests/cli/` tree arrives with M8), so both upstream test hunks were placed in the fork's existing `tests/cli_wrapper.rs`: `run_shell_hook` is now a thin wrapper over a new `run_shell_hook_with_env(asset, args, input, extra_env)` that scrubs `CODEX_THREAD_ID` before applying the caller's env, and `codex_hook_reports_session_id_from_stdin` becomes `codex_hook_reports_persisted_root_session_and_ignores_ephemeral_or_nested_sessions` covering all four upstream cases (persisted root reports, matching inherited id still reports, null transcript is ignored, mismatched inherited id is ignored). **EXCLUDED:** the `assets/codex/*.ps1` hunk of `1491b7dd` (no `.ps1` twins in this Linux-only fork, ADR 0013) — no other Windows-path hunk exists in these three commits, since `a07871a5`'s Windows support is the portable TypeScript change itself. |
| *(this commit)* | `d742e515` *(**post-relicense** — `NOTICE` updated)* | **Installing or removing the claude hook stops reformatting the user's `~/.claude/settings.json`.** The old `install_claude`/`uninstall_claude` did a full `serde_json::Value` round-trip and wrote the result with `to_string_pretty`, which destroyed the user's indentation, key order (serde's `Map` is a `BTreeMap` — no `preserve_order` feature — so every object came back alphabetised), inline/compact layout, CRLF line endings, number and escape literals (`1e+02` → `100.0`, `a` → `a`) and the trailing newline, and churned the file on every install even when nothing changed. New fork-adopted `src/integration/claude_settings.rs` (804 lines, upstream verbatim except the one test fixture path `herdr-agent-state.sh` → `zynk-agent-state.sh`) replaces that with a two-track edit: **track A** builds the intended result as a `serde_json::Value` using the pre-existing `config_edit` helpers and returns the input byte-for-byte when it already equals the parsed original (so a repeat `zynk integration install claude` writes nothing at all), and **track B** applies the same edit surgically to the source text through `jsonc-parser`'s trivia-owning mutable CST (`CstNode::remove()` / `CstObject::append`), so every byte the edit does not touch is re-emitted unchanged. Containers whose direct children hold no newline bail out of the CST — whose `append` would inject newline+indent formatting and shred a one-line file — into a hand-rolled byte splice (`append_hooks_property_compact` / `append_session_property_compact` / `append_session_entry_compact`) that reuses the file's own inferred key/value separator and inter-item delimiter. Every emitted string then passes `verify_updated`, which re-parses it with `serde_json` and demands equality with the desired `Value`, so a splice bug surfaces as a clean `io::Error` instead of a corrupted user file. The ten install-side and ten uninstall-side `remove_hook_commands` calls collapse into one shared nine-entry `HOOK_REMOVALS` table (`SessionStart` carries `["idle", "session"]`) — set-equal to the old calls, order-irrelevant. `src/integration/targets.rs` keeps only the read/delegate/compare-and-write shell; `src/integration/mod.rs` gains `mod claude_settings;`. New dependency `jsonc-parser = { version = "0.33.1", default-features = false, features = ["cst", "serde_json"] }` (`Cargo.toml`, verbatim upstream line); its only dependency is `serde_json`, which is already a direct fork dep, so `Cargo.lock` grows by exactly the ten lines upstream's own lock grew by (the package stanza plus the `zynk` dep-list entry, same `0.33.1` checksum) and the single `libsqlite3-sys` node ADR 0006 depends on is untouched. **Behaviour changes worth knowing:** the canonical `SessionStart` entry now keeps its position instead of being removed and re-appended at the tail; a duplicate key anywhere in the document is now a hard error (`contains duplicate key "…"`) where `serde_json` silently kept the last and rewrote a deduped file, and that check runs only when an edit is actually needed; and a fresh install on a machine with no `settings.json` seeds `"{}"`, takes the compact path and writes a minified one-line file with no trailing newline where the fork used to write 2-space pretty. **Comments are still rejected, not preserved** — despite the crate name, `parse_value` is plain `serde_json::from_str` and `strict_parse_options()` turns all seven `jsonc-parser` leniencies off (`allow_comments: false` included), so a JSONC `settings.json` fails install exactly as it did before; the new fork test `install_claude_rejects_settings_with_comments_and_leaves_the_file_untouched` pins that and pins that the user's file is left byte-for-byte alone when it happens. Tests: the eight upstream `claude_settings::tests` are adopted verbatim and pass unmodified, and the fork adds `install_then_uninstall_claude_restores_original_settings_bytes` — a real `install_claude()` + `uninstall_claude()` round trip under an isolated `HOME` over a 4-space-indented, non-alphabetically-ordered fixture, asserting the untouched prefix and suffix survive byte-for-byte, that `zeta` still precedes `hooks` still precedes `alpha`, and that the file returns to its **exact original bytes** after the uninstall. The five pre-existing claude settings tests pass unchanged. **EXCLUDED:** the `remove_legacy_bash_hook_file(&hook_path)?` call that appears in upstream's post-image at `targets.rs:135`/`:554` — it is diff *context*, not part of this change, and the function does not exist in this fork (it is upstream's Windows `.sh` → `.ps1` cutover helper, dropped in M1). No version bump: `CLAUDE_INTEGRATION_VERSION` stays `7` — the hook asset is untouched, only the installer's write path changed. `Cargo.lock` is deliberately **not** added to the `NOTICE` §4(b) list: its added stanza is cargo-generated resolution metadata, not upstream code. |
| *(this commit)* | `50ddc06f` *(portable half; post-relicense — `NOTICE` §4(b))* | **Hermes reports session identity by shelling out to the zynk CLI instead of speaking the socket protocol itself, and every pane learns the path of the running binary.** `src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py`: `_base_params`/`_send` (raw `AF_UNIX` socket, hand-built JSON-RPC envelope, `json`/`random`/`socket` imports) are replaced by `_pane_id`/`_send_session(session_id, start_source)`, which runs `$ZYNK_BIN_PATH pane report-agent-session <pane> --source zynk:hermes --agent hermes --seq <ns> --agent-session-id <id> --session-start-source <src>` through `subprocess.run(check=False, timeout=1)` with both streams to `DEVNULL`, still swallowing every exception so a hook can never break the agent. `_report_session` and the three registered hooks (`on_session_start`, `on_session_reset`, `pre_llm_call`) are unchanged, so hermes stays session-identity-only: it reports identity, never lifecycle state, and its receipts still anchor on `hook_identity`. **`HERMES_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 3 -> 4** with the asset marker `# ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=4` in the same commit (upstream is 4 -> 5; the fork is one revision behind because `e608a751`, which took hermes 3 -> 4 upstream, is deferred past M8 — const and marker still agree, which is what `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` enforces). **`src/integration/env.rs`:** `apply_pane_base_env` now also exports `ZYNK_BIN_PATH` from `std::env::current_exe()`, so a hook asset invokes the running build rather than whatever `zynk` a `PATH` lookup finds (previously only plugin panes got it, via `src/app/api/plugins/{panes,runtime}.rs` and `src/app/input/navigate.rs`); new `HERMES_HOME_ENV_VAR = "HERMES_HOME"` and `hermes_dir()` honours it through `expand_tilde_path` before falling back to `~/.hermes` — hermes' own variable, deliberately not renamed to a `ZYNK_*` name, exactly like `OMP_CONFIG_DIR_ENV_VAR`/`GROK_HOME_ENV_VAR`. **`src/integration/targets.rs`:** `install_cursor`/`uninstall_cursor`/`install_mastracode`/`uninstall_mastracode` drop their local `shell_single_quote` + `format!("bash {…} {action}")` and call the shared `hook_command(&hook_path, Some(action))`, which emits the byte-identical string on Linux — a de-duplication, not a behaviour change. **`src/detect/mod.rs`:** `agent_name_from_known_package_path` gains the 4-component window `["node_modules","mastracode","dist","cli"]`, so a globally npm-installed mastracode running as `node …/dist/cli.js` is identified from its package path; covered by the new `identify_agent_in_job_detects_node_wrapped_mastracode_package_cli` (Linux npm prefix, not upstream's Windows `%APPDATA%` path, whose fixture also embedded the forbidden upstream name). **Tests:** `scripts/test_hermes_integration_asset.py` now drives `_send_session`, adds `test_send_session_uses_cli_with_the_active_pane` (argv prefix `[$ZYNK_BIN_PATH, "pane", "report-agent-session", "w1:p2"]`, `check=False`) and the fork-added `test_send_session_stays_silent_outside_a_zynk_pane`; `apply_pane_base_env_exports_the_running_binary_path` and `hermes_dir_honors_the_hermes_home_override` are new; `clear_integration_path_env` now clears `HERMES_HOME` so a stray value cannot redirect an install test; `install_cursor_writes_hook_and_updates_hooks_json` and `install_mastracode_writes_hook_and_updates_hooks_json` assert exact equality against `hook_command` instead of the old prefix/suffix substring checks. **EXCLUDED (linux-only fork):** the four new `.ps1` assets (cursor, devin, grok, mastracode) and the `cfg!(windows)` `*_HOOK_INSTALL_NAME`/`*_HOOK_ASSET` selectors that reference them; the `cursor-agent.cmd` selection in `src/agent_resume.rs` and `interactive_agent_executable`; the `#[cfg(windows)]` `LOCALAPPDATA`/`USERPROFILE` branch of `hermes_dir`; the `mastracode_hook_command` and `grok_hook_command` wrappers plus their PowerShell `-EncodedCommand` bodies and the migration-only duplicate `remove_flat_command_hook` calls (on Linux each collapses to the plain `hook_command` already emitted); `integration_target_supported` and `hermes_install_layout_available`'s Windows probe, neither of which exists in this fork; every `#[cfg(windows)]` test; and `docs/next/**`. **Sheet deviation:** §2.30 said to keep the asset's `if os.name == "nt": creationflags = CREATE_NO_WINDOW` branch and its guarded test assertion; both are dropped, because M4-07 (`dae0fd7`) already set the precedent of stripping Windows branches out of the *assets* as well as the Rust — no fork asset carries a platform branch, and the guarded assertion would pin behaviour the asset deliberately no longer has. |
| *(this commit)* | `ccccda54` *(**post-relicense** — `NOTICE` updated)* | **An opencode pane now tracks the root conversation selected in ITS OWN TUI, and stops adopting the activity of clients attached to the same server; `OPENCODE_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 9 -> 10 on BOTH opencode assets.** One opencode server is shared by every client attached to it, so `session.created` and every `chat.message`/`session.status` the bundled server plugin sees are server-GLOBAL: the old asset read a root `session.created` as this pane's own new session and reported it as `session_start_source: "new"`, which let an attached client repoint the pane's identity, and a report naming another root session still moved the pane's state. **Asset half:** `assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.js` drops the start source from `reportSession` and, on `session.created`, only records `reportedRootSessionID` locally; a NEW second asset `assets/opencode/zynk-tui-session.js` (108 lines) runs in opencode's `tui` slot, polls `api.route.current` every 100 ms and reports the selected ROOT session (`session.parentID` absent) as `pane.report_agent_session` with `session_start_source: "select"` and NO `seq`, retrying at 0/100/400/1000 ms while the same route stays selected so a selection made before zynk detects the process is not lost. **Rust half:** `session_start_source_allows_session_replacement` swaps opencode's arm `Some("new")` -> `Some("select")`, `normalize_session_start_source` accepts `"select"`, and a new `is_unsequenced_opencode_selection` exempts the TUI shape from the shared sequence fence — both plugins report under `zynk:opencode` but only the server plugin numbers its reports, so `accept_hook_report(source, None)` would refuse every selection once the server had reported once. **Second installed artifact.** `integration_specs()` keys ONE path per target, so opencode's completeness is checked from the registered plugin path: `install_opencode` also writes `zynk-tui-session.js` beside the config dir and registers `./zynk-tui-session.js` in opencode's own `tui.jsonc`; new `src/integration/opencode_config.rs` (298 lines) does that edit through the `jsonc-parser` CST that M4-12 already vendored, so the user's comments, formatting, trailing commas and existing plugin entries survive, an existing entry is never duplicated, and a `["spec", {..}]` option tuple counts as configured. `registry::integration_status_at` gains an opencode arm mirroring the existing grok one: a current server plugin beside a missing/stale/unregistered TUI plugin is `Outdated`, and `uninstall_opencode` removes both files plus the managed config entry, collecting errors so a broken `tui.jsonc` still gets the plugins deleted. Because that arm can now mark an install at the expected version `Outdated`, `zynk integration status` would have printed the nonsense `outdated (v10 < v10)`; `src/cli/integration.rs` gains upstream's guarded arm printing `needs repair (v10)` instead (it covers grok's identical case too). **IDENTITY REVIEW — every new `src/terminal/state.rs` path.** Three hunks, all in functions this fork owns; `hook_authority` is the only authority read and no path consults `effective_agent_label()`/`detected_agent` for IDENTITY. (1) The `("zynk:opencode","opencode",Some("select"))` replacement arm is a pure function of `(source, agent_label, session_start_source)`, all three off the hook report; the pair is already in `crate::detect::full_lifecycle_hook_authority`, so it cannot widen a session-identity-only owner, and it NARROWS what may repoint the pane (`new` no longer qualifies). (2) `is_unsequenced_opencode_selection` is a pure function of the four values the report itself carries and gates only `accept_hook_report`; it leaves the identity-only retirement gate, `known_agent_label_conflicts_with_detected_agent`, both owner-conflict gates and the `replacing_identity_only_session && !process_present` clamp untouched, and it never records a sequence (there is none to record), so the server plugin's own numbering is unchanged — asserted by `opencode_tui_selection_is_exempt_from_the_shared_sequence_fence`. (3) `opencode_state_report_is_cross_talk` is upstream's `opencode_cross_talk`, placed in `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` BEFORE `hook_report_survives_retirement` and `accept_hook_report` so an ignored cross-talk report consumes no sequence, exactly as upstream's early `FullLifecycleHookReportRoute::Ignore`. It resolves the anchor through the existing `owned_session_ref` (`hook_authority` first, then the persisted session — which is what a TUI selection leaves behind), and reads `detected_agent` ONLY as upstream's `process_present` evidence that the agent's own process is in the foreground, the identical use `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` already makes of it; identity itself still comes off the hook. It is inert for every other owner (`(source, agent_label) != ("zynk:opencode","opencode")` returns early) and for identity-only owners, which never reach this function. No `hook_identity` branch, retirement/evidence guard or suppression-boundary path is touched. **EXCLUDED, with reasons.** `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md` (standing). The new asset's `process.platform === "win32"` named-pipe branch and its `socketEndpoint` indirection — linux-only fork, and the M4-05/M4-07 precedent already strips platform branches out of the *assets*; `net.createConnection(socketPath, ...)` matches the sibling opencode asset exactly. Upstream's `session_start_source_is_recognized` (+`"select"`) and the whole `full_lifecycle_source && unsequenced_selection` suppression stash (`SuppressedFullLifecycleHookReport::{replacement_session_ref, pending_replacement_report}`, and the `selection_can_reconcile` clearing of it) have NO fork counterpart and are not needed: upstream REFUSES a full-lifecycle session-start report while `!process_present || generation_gated || !session_anchored` and therefore has to park the selection until detection catches up, whereas this fork's `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` has no process gate on that path at all — the selection anchors immediately and detection then finds it already in place. Same end state, proved by `opencode_tui_selection_anchors_before_the_process_is_detected`, and the divergence is recorded there in a comment. This fork's suppression map is `SuppressedHookReport` (shared with `hook_identity`) plus `stale_hook_sessions`, with no replacement/pending fields, so the stash could not have been taken verbatim in any case. **Divergent upstream assertions not adopted:** upstream's `opencode_tui_selection_reanchors_full_lifecycle_authority` asserts `hook_report_sequences["herdr:opencode"] == 20` after the reanchor, because its routing returns `Ignore` before the fence records the attached client's `seq: 21`; this fork records the sequence in `accept_hook_report` before `live_full_lifecycle_hook_authority_conflicts_with_session` rejects, a pre-existing ordering difference this commit does not introduce, so the fork's adaptation asserts the behaviour that matters (the late old and late attached reports are both refused and the state stays `Working`) and drops the sequence assertion. **Tests.** `src/integration/tests.rs`: `install_opencode_writes_plugin_to_plugins_dir` grows the two new artifacts and the `tui.jsonc` entry; `uninstall_opencode_removes_plugin_when_present` is replaced by `uninstall_opencode_removes_plugins_and_managed_tui_config_entry`; new `opencode_status_requires_the_tui_plugin_and_config_entry`, `install_opencode_invalid_tui_config_does_not_write_plugins` and `uninstall_opencode_removes_plugins_when_tui_config_is_invalid`; the all-agent parity table gains an `opencode_tui` row so BOTH markers are pinned to the one const. `src/integration/opencode_config.rs` carries upstream's four CST tests (temp prefix `zynk-opencode-config-`, test-local spec literal `./zynk-tui-state.js` kept deliberately distinct from `OPENCODE_TUI_PLUGIN_SPEC`). `src/terminal/state.rs`: `opencode_new_session_ref_replaces_existing_session_ref` becomes `opencode_tui_selection_replaces_existing_session_ref`, plus upstream's `opencode_server_{new,resume}_does_not_replace_existing_session_ref` and fork-shaped `opencode_tui_selection_{is_exempt_from_the_shared_sequence_fence,anchors_before_the_process_is_detected,reanchors_full_lifecycle_authority}` and `opencode_state_report_for_another_root_session_is_ignored`. Bun: the new 181-line `assets/opencode/zynk-tui-session.test.ts` (5 tests) is wired as the fourth line of `test-ts`, and `assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` takes upstream's two new tests. `hook_authority_preserves_current_session_ref_when_incoming_ref_differs` still passes unchanged: it drives `zynk:opencode` with NO detected agent, so the new guard's `process_present` requirement leaves it on the old clamp path. **Red proofs, each reverted and restored, file re-verified byte-identical:** reverting the `select` arm to `new` fails 4 state tests; dropping the `!unsequenced_selection &&` fence bypass fails 3; dropping the `opencode_state_report_is_cross_talk` call fails 2; holding the const at 9 fails `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` (`opencode asset version …`, left `Some(10)` right `Some(9)`) and holding the TUI marker at 9 fails the new row (`opencode_tui asset version …`, left `Some(9)` right `Some(10)`); dropping the registry completeness arm fails `opencode_status_requires_the_tui_plugin_and_config_entry` (left `Current`, right `Outdated`); restoring the `session.created` report fails the new Bun test `does not classify server-global root creation as a local selection`; | *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix; corrects the M4-05 row's claim)* | **An opencode child session's permission/question prompt now reports under the ROOT session that owns it, instead of under no session at all; `OPENCODE_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 10 -> 11 on BOTH opencode assets.** Codex M4 P2 (`msg_a12f9abba8700337`), consolidated verdict `msg_9f7689e31fce8932` + anchor correction `msg_598a856373689990`; the exact `file:line` anchors below are Codex's and supersede the approximate ranges in this commit's brief. **A corrected M4-05 claim.** The `be901fea` `60c04005` `f23e3779` row above closes with "the child-prompt path deliberately omits `agent_session_id` precisely so the stored session triple and the owner-coherence check keep the root session". That is upstream's intent, but it is **wrong for this fork's Rust side**. The asset's child branch at `src/integration/assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.js:139-146` (at `d1ef977`) stores only the child id, DROPS the `parentID` it was just handed, and calls `reportState(state)` with no session at all. `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` then takes that missing `session_ref` as the owner's session outright: `src/terminal/state.rs:536-544` clears `persisted_agent_session` and installs a `HookAuthority` whose `session_ref` is `None`, after which `owned_session_ref` (`:770`) returns `None`. A session-less full-lifecycle report therefore REPLACES the pane's session anchor rather than leaving it alone. Two consequences, both real: (a) a child `permission.asked` under the pane's OWN root erases that root's session anchor, so a message addressed to it just before the prompt can no longer be receipted — `zynk.message_received` answers `receiver_identity_mismatch`; (b) a child of ANOTHER attached client's root bypasses `opencode_state_report_is_cross_talk`, whose whole comparison is `owned_session_ref(...).zip(session_ref.as_ref()).is_some_and(|(anchored, incoming)| &anchored != incoming)` (`src/terminal/state.rs:1105-1107`) — `zip` yields `None` when either side is absent, so a session-less report can never compare unequal and is never dropped; the pane flips Idle -> Blocked, undoing exactly what the `ccccda54` row above installed. **Why M4-05's own proof missed it:** that row's runtime proof drove the installed asset against a real socket and asserted on the SIX REQUESTS THE ASSET EMITTED — "a child `permission.asked` `blocked` and a child `question.replied` `working` both with no `agent_session_id`" — a faithful reading of the wire that says nothing about what the server then did to the pane's stored session. Codex caught it by carrying the same asset one step further: import the real `src/integration/assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.js` under `bun -e` against an isolated server socket, then read `pane.get` and attempt the receipt. **Fix (asset-side only).** The `childSessions` Set becomes a `childParents` Map (child id -> parent id), filled at the same `info?.id && info.parentID` site that previously discarded the parent; new `rootSessionIDFor(sessionID)` walks the parent links upward behind a `visited` Set and returns the topmost KNOWN ancestor (the first id that is not itself a tracked child), or `undefined` on a cycle; the child branch resolves that root and reports `await reportState(state, root)`, sending NOTHING when the root is unresolvable — never a session-less report again. An ancestry that runs past what this process has seen yields that ancestor as-is, which costs at most a report the `:1105-1107` guard filters, never a stolen anchor. `chat.message`'s child guard moves from `.has` on the Set to `.has` on the Map (same predicate). **The Rust side is deliberately NOT changed:** a session-less full-lifecycle report still replaces the anchor; that is the contract, and the asset now respects it. Every other asset path is byte-identical — the `requestChain` serialization, `SESSION_STATE_BY_STATUS`, the root switch, and the `reportedRootSessionID` bookkeeping (`reportState` still records whatever session it reported under, so a child report of a foreign root now updates it exactly as a root-level foreign `session.status` already did; the Rust guard, not this variable, is the filter). **Observed and deliberately NOT changed, for the reviewer:** `session.error` arrives server-global without a `sessionID`, so its root-level `reportState("blocked", undefined)` hits the identical `:536-544` contract and clears the anchor the same way — outside this P2's scope, left as it stands. The `childParents` Map, like the Set before it, is never pruned on `session.deleted` (upstream behaviour, unchanged). **Version parity:** `OPENCODE_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 10 -> 11 (`src/integration/mod.rs:129`) with the `// ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=` marker moved in BOTH `zynk-agent-state.js` and `zynk-tui-session.js`, which `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` enforces in both directions. As the M4-05 row already recorded, `src/integration/tests.rs` needs no edit: its two opencode rows (`:2914`, `:2921`) and `opencode_status_requires_the_tui_plugin_and_config_entry` (`:2406`) all read the const, and a grep across `src/` and `tests/` for a hardcoded opencode version marker now matches nothing. **`NOTICE` unchanged:** every touched file that carries post-`cd5ea1be` upstream code — `src/integration/assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.js`, its `.test.ts`, `zynk-tui-session.js`, `src/integration/mod.rs` and `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` — is ALREADY in the `<!-- apache-modified-files -->` list (`NOTICE:71-74`, `:82`, `:93`), so there is nothing to append. **Tests, RED first.** Bun (`zynk-agent-state.test.ts`, 7 -> 10 tests): `reports child prompts without replacing the root session` is renamed `reports child prompts under the owning root session` and its five `agent_session_id` expectations move `undefined` -> `"root-session"`; three new tests cover a grandchild (`reports a nested child's prompt under its root`), a foreign root (`does not attribute another root's child prompt to the last reported root` — the request must carry `other-root`, not the `root-session` the pane last reported), and a cycle (`sends nothing for a child whose ancestry is cyclic` — zero requests). On `d1ef977` all four fail (6 pass, 4 fail): the five/one/one session ids read `undefined` where `root-session`/`root-session`/`other-root` is expected, and the cyclic child emits one `pane.report_agent` `blocked` where none is allowed. Rust end-to-end (`tests/zynk_receipt.rs`, +3): `opencode_child_prompt_through_the_real_asset(parent, nested)` transcribes Codex's probe without its `review_` prefix. It anchors a detected opencode pane on `visible-root`, then — the control that makes the RED meaningful — sends a first message and asserts its receipt is already `received` ("initial root receipt must succeed"), proving the anchor was VALID before the replay; only then does it submit the second message, drive the REAL bundled asset under `bun -e` (`ZYNK_ENV=1`, `ZYNK_PANE_ID`, `ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`) so a child of `parent` asks a permission, and return `pane.get` plus the second receipt. Both the own-root and the foreign-root tests keep that control. `an_own_opencode_child_prompt_keeps_the_root_receipt_anchor` and `a_nested_own_opencode_child_prompt_keeps_the_root_receipt_anchor` assert the pane reads `blocked` AND the receipt is `received`; `another_clients_opencode_child_prompt_leaves_the_pane_alone` asserts the pane stays `idle`. All three fail on `d1ef977` — the two own-root probes with `receiver_identity_mismatch` (`left: Null`, `right: "received"`), the foreign one with `left: Some(String("blocked"))`, `right: Some(String("idle"))`. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=~/workspace/.zynk-verify-target` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and a short `TMPDIR` for the SUN_LEN budget):** `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `just test-ts` 35/35 across four files (up from 32); `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` 157/157 (unchanged); `--bin zynk -E 'test(/terminal::state::tests::/)'` 124/124; `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 52/52 twice (49 -> 52); the full `--bin zynk` suite 2701/2701 passed, 1 skipped (unchanged — this commit adds no Rust unit test); `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_agent_detection_manifest_check scripts.test_conventional_commits` 12/12; `just gate` clean. |

## M4 closure note (Codex Gate-2 APPROVE at `38e039b`, `msg_97d75b0fe34b71a0`)

Three facts survive M4's approval. **(1) No-amend-rule deviation, recorded not repeated:** M4-17 was first
committed as `517ac9e` and amended to `38e039b` before any review packet named it; the amend changed one
sentence of a ledger row and nothing else — `git diff --quiet 517ac9e 38e039b -- src tests` passes, so `src/`
and `tests/` are byte-identical across the two and the only delta is `docs/zynk/fork-patch-ledger.md`
(1 insertion, 1 deletion). Every follow-up from here is a NEW commit. **(2) OPEN, nonblocking, carried to B2:**
an opencode `session.error` that arrives without a `sessionID`
(`src/integration/assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.js:207-208`) still reaches `reportState("blocked",
sessionID)` with a falsy id, and `reportState` (`:132-138`) then omits `agent_session_id` altogether — an
unanchored root-level report. It is pre-existing at `d03c717` and the Gate-2 approval did not fix it.
**(3) Precision to apply in the next opencode-asset pass:** the asset comment at `:112-117` overstates its own
guarantee. `rootSessionIDFor` returns the first ancestor that is not itself a tracked child, returned as-is
when the chain runs past what the process has seen, and only a cycle returns `undefined` and is suppressed;
the "costs a filtered report rather than a stolen anchor" claim therefore rests on the server-side cross-talk
guard at `src/terminal/state.rs:1100-1107`, which drops a mismatched incoming ref ONLY when the pane has
already detected OpenCode (`process_present`) and holds an `owned_session_ref` to compare against. Absent
either, the guard returns `false` and drops nothing — it is a conditional filter, not an unconditional
fail-closed guarantee.

# LINUX-ONLY + v0.8.2 PORT LEDGER — M5 runtime-authority refactor, app state and UI

M5 begins here. The upstream `v0.7.1..v0.8.2` runtime-authority chain routes every TUI mutation through
the same socket handlers the API calls, so a keypress and a `zynk` command take one code path. The chain
`1a4e94e5 -> 8b74fb8a -> 97f78226 -> 9a91a2ac -> 0bab0157 -> edd08e89 -> c97e0986 -> 89fe8975` is
reviewed as ONE unit ending at `89fe8975`; a row below on its own is only the seam, not the finished
behaviour. The licensing split of the M2 section above still applies: an upstream commit dated before
`cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22) arrives under AGPL-3.0-or-later, a later one under Apache-2.0, and only the
latter appends its touched files to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list.

| zynk | upstream | what / where / why / adaptations |
|---|---|---|
| *(this commit)* | `1a4e94e5` *(pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **Feature (authority seam): the TUI stops mutating `AppState` inline and dispatches the same `Method` the socket does, so keyboard, mouse and API share one mutation path.** Mechanism: two new App entry points (`src/app/api.rs:28` `dispatch_tui_api_request` → `handle_api_request`; `:39` `dispatch_tui_deferred_api_request` → `handle_deferred_worktree_api_request` over a local `mpsc` channel, `try_recv` for the immediate rejection only), four new socket methods, and rewritten input paths that call handlers instead of `AppState` methods. **Wire contract (ADDITIVE only, no existing `serde(rename)` touched):** `workspace.move` (`src/api/schema.rs:75`), `tab.move` (`:97`), `layout.set_split_ratio` (`:133`), `pane.focus` (`:149`) — params `WorkspaceMoveParams` (`src/api/schema/workspaces.rs:22`), `TabMoveParams` (`src/api/schema/tabs.rs:30`), `LayoutSetSplitRatioParams` (`src/api/schema/panes.rs:128`, beside `LayoutApplyParams` as the fork's module split dictates), `pane.focus` reuses `PaneTarget`. All four are compiler-forced into `api_method_name` (`src/api/server.rs:289,300,318,326`), the App dispatch (`src/app/api.rs:853,887,907,919`) and `request_changes_ui` (`src/api/mod.rs:46,54,57,67`). **Handlers:** `handle_workspace_move` (`src/app/api/workspaces.rs:120`, `workspace_move_failed` on out-of-bounds `insert_index`), `handle_tab_move` (`src/app/api/tabs.rs:172`, `tab_move_failed`, refreshes the tab bar only when the target workspace is active), `handle_layout_set_split_ratio` (`src/app/api/layouts.rs:217`, `invalid_ratio` on a non-finite ratio, `split_not_found` when the path names no split), `handle_pane_focus` (`src/app/api/panes.rs:154`, focuses across tabs AND workspaces then forces `Mode::Terminal`). `TileLayout::set_ratio_at` now returns `bool` (`src/layout.rs:209`) so the handler can distinguish "no such split" from "set" — the recursive `set_ratio_at` (`:580`) returns `false` on a non-`Split` node. **Modal parity in the deferred worktree path** (`src/app/api/worktrees/deferred.rs:377,439,513,571`): a create/remove that fails now restores the TUI modal (`creating`/`removing` cleared, `error` set, `dirty_worktree_requires_force` promoted to `force_confirmation` once), and one that succeeds closes it — the modal is no longer left spinning when the git work is done off the main loop. **Input rewrites:** `src/app/input/mod.rs` routes rename/resize/confirm-close/context-menu keys to new `*_via_api` App methods and pre-focuses the pressed pane through `pane.focus` for intentional left/middle presses in `Terminal`/`Resize`; `AppState::handle_mouse` (`src/app/input/mouse.rs:96`) now returns the new `MouseAction` enum (`:29`) instead of `Option<SettingsAction>` so focus/move/split-ratio decisions leave `AppState` un-mutated and the App performs them via the socket, with `handle_mobile_mouse` (`:1081`) returning the new `MobileMouseResult` three-way (`Ignored`/`Consumed`/`Action`) because a mobile tap must be able to carry an action out; `AppState::focus_pane` (`:1394`) becomes a no-op since focus is now the API's job; `src/app/input/modal.rs:903-1261` adds the `impl App` block (`handle_rename_key_via_api`, `save_rename_modal_via_api`, `handle_resize_key_via_api`, `handle_confirm_close_key_via_api`, `handle_context_menu_key_via_api`, `apply_context_menu_action_via_api`) plus the free `cancel_rename_modal`; `src/app/input/navigate.rs:102-686` adds `execute_tui_navigate_action` and the sixteen `*_via_api` helpers plus `navigate_reserved_action_for_key` and `api_pane_direction`; `src/app/input/terminal.rs:48` and the two `handle_prefix_key`/`handle_navigate_key` call sites now use `execute_tui_navigate_action`; `src/app/mod.rs:858,873,898,917,923,929` turn the `request_new_workspace` / `request_new_tab` / `request_new_workspace_cwd` / `request_submit_worktree_*` flags into `workspace.create` / `tab.create` / `worktree.create` / `worktree.open` / `worktree.remove` dispatches; `src/app/worktrees.rs:543,662,697,881` add `submit_worktree_create_via_api` / `submit_worktree_open_via_api` / `submit_worktree_remove_via_api` plus the free `immediate_api_error_message`. Three `AppState` items gain `pub(crate)` for the new callers (`src/app/actions.rs:260,1303,1474`: `pane_focus_target_indices`, `ensure_agent_panel_entry_visible`, `refresh_tab_bar_view`) and `AppState::move_tab` is DELETED — its only caller was the mouse drag-end that now dispatches `tab.move` (`Workspace::move_tab` stays). Upstream's `handle_navigate_reserved_key` behaviour change is carried as-is: the new predicate returns `None` for `workspace_up`/`workspace_down` so those keys no longer move the sidebar selection from the reserved path (the old free function stays for the AppState-only test harness). **Adaptations vs upstream:** (1) every upstream `env: Default::default()` on `WorkspaceCreateParams` / `TabCreateParams` / `PaneSplitParams` is DROPPED — those structs have no `env` field in this fork (`src/api/schema/{workspaces,tabs,panes}.rs`), which is a v0.8.2 field the fork has not ported; (2) the `#[cfg(windows)]` dirty-checkout pre-check in `submit_worktree_remove_via_api` is DROPPED per ADR 0013 (Linux-only, no `#[cfg(target_os)]` branches) — consistent with the fork already having dropped the same block from `start_api_worktree_remove`; (4) upstream's remove-success modal reset sits after `emit_worktree_removed_event`, which this fork never ported (no such `EventKind`), so it lands immediately before the success response is encoded (`src/app/api/worktrees/deferred.rs:571`); (5) upstream's `src/api/schema/tests.rs` does not exist here — its `authority_mutation_requests_round_trip` is retargeted into `src/api/schema.rs` `mod tests`; (6) the identity/rebrand renames in the ported test bodies (`herdr` → `zynk` repo paths and labels). **Invariants checked:** `AppState`/`PaneState` stay pure data — `src/app/state.rs` and `src/pane/state.rs` are untouched and `AppState::test_new()` still has 95 call sites across 15 files that compile (a leaked channel would break all 95); `src/ui.rs` is untouched, so `render(&AppState, frame)` gained no mutation; `src/app/creation.rs` is untouched, so `authoritative_receiver_identity` is byte-identical (32 lines, sha256 `9430e4c6ec415e59…`); the `AppEvent::HookStateReported` identity region in `src/app/actions.rs` is byte-identical (81-line window, sha256 `dca15a8882ce3742…`); fork-owned `src/app/api/zynk.rs` and the CLI send path (`src/cli/{pane,agent,native}.rs`) are untouched and `zynk.message_received` still routes; the ordered `Method` wire-id list changed by exactly four insertions and no rename. **Tests (RED first):** the new fork-only characterization test `method_wire_ids_are_stable` (`src/api/schema.rs:790`) pins the FULL ordered `Method` → wire-id contract (all 80 ids, including the four new ones and fork-owned `zynk.message_received`) against the list `serde` itself reports for an unknown tag, so a rename, reorder, addition or removal fails the test; `authority_mutation_method_wire_ids_are_pinned` (`:802`) names the four new variants by path so a removal is a compile error; `authority_mutation_requests_round_trip` (`:855`) mirrors upstream's round trip. Plus eight ported handler tests: `layout_set_split_ratio_{updates_existing_split,rejects_missing_split}`, `api_pane_focus_{focuses_direct_target_across_tabs_and_workspaces,rejects_invalid_pane_id}`, `api_tab_move_reorders_tabs_in_target_workspace`, `api_workspace_move_reorders_workspaces`, `submit_worktree_{create,remove}_via_api_restores_modal_on_immediate_rejection`. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`/`ZYNK_*`/`XDG_*`/`TMPDIR`):** `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` exit 0 (no orphaned dead code — `AppState::move_tab` was deleted rather than allowed); `--bin zynk -E 'test(/api::schema/)'` 30/30 (27 -> 30), `-E 'test(/zynk::header/)'` 28/28, `-E 'test(/zynk::receipt/)'` 29/29, `-E 'test(/protocol::wire/)'` 47/47, `-E 'test(/app::/)'` 603/603, the delivery-transition + body-purity set (`delivery_transition_validation_allows_only_m2_transitions`, `delivery_transition_validation_covers_m3a_received_matrix`, `failed_outcome_claims_no_delivery`, `zynk::message`, `zynk::persistence`) 51/51; `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 52/52; the full `--bin zynk` suite 2712/2712 passed, 1 skipped (2701 -> 2712, +11 = the eleven new tests); per-file `#[test]`+`#[tokio::test]` counts show no decrease and `src/` totals 2702 -> 2713; `just gate` clean (tracked-path 1693 files, scrub 82 files, gitleaks no leaks). |
| *(this commit)* | `8b74fb8a` *(pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **Feature (placement events): a workspace or tab that actually moves now emits a `workspace.moved` / `tab.moved` event carrying the reordered list, so a socket client no longer has to re-poll `workspace.list` / `tab.list` after a drag or a `move` command.** Second link of the M5 chain, on top of the `1a4e94e5` seam: the TUI drag now routes through `workspace.move` / `tab.move`, so the event fires for keyboard, mouse and API alike. **Wire contract (ADDITIVE only, no existing `serde(rename)` touched):** two new `EventKind`s — `WorkspaceMoved` (`src/api/schema/events.rs:182`, dot name `workspace.moved` at `:206`) and `TabMoved` (`:187`, `tab.moved` at `:211`) — with the matching `Subscription::WorkspaceMoved` (`:25`) / `Subscription::TabMoved` (`:39`), `EventMatch::WorkspaceMoved` (`:115`) / `EventMatch::TabMoved` (`:135`), `EventData::WorkspaceMoved { workspace_id, insert_index, workspaces }` (`:325`) / `EventData::TabMoved { tab_id, workspace_id, insert_index, tabs }` (`:345`), both added to `PLUGIN_HOOK_EVENT_KINDS` (`:233`, `:238`) so plugins can hook them, and both wired into `ActiveSubscription::new` (`src/api/subscriptions.rs:123`, `:151`). One new response variant `ResponseResult::LayoutSplitRatioSet { layout }` (`src/api/schema/response.rs:148`): `layout.set_split_ratio` stops borrowing `layout_apply`'s response type and answers with its own (`src/app/api/layouts.rs:246`). The `Method` wire-id list is UNCHANGED — this commit adds no method. **`PROTOCOL_VERSION` 14 -> 15** (`src/protocol/wire.rs:16`); the fork's stale ordering test is renamed to match (`client_message_wire_tags_preserve_protocol_15_order`, `:858`), keeping its tag-ordering assertion, and `tests/support/mod.rs:18` gains `pub const CURRENT_PROTOCOL: u32 = 15` so the client-handshake integration tests read the version from one place instead of eleven literals. **`PROTOCOL_VERSION` policy for the rest of the program (port sheet §7.2):** upstream bumps the constant at `8b74fb8a` (15, here), `2bc1724c` (16), `e0758c32` (17), `e7fc85bf` (18), `b76adc15` (19) and `6f311498` (20). `2bc1724c` is EXCLUDED from the port and is never taken, so the fork treats `PROTOCOL_VERSION` as MONOTONE — the max value reached by the commits actually ported, not a replay of every upstream bump. M5 therefore goes 14 -> 15 here and -> 19 at M5-24 (`b76adc15`), and **M8 must DROP the 17 and 18 bumps** (`e0758c32`, `e7fc85bf` — both already below the value M5 leaves behind) **and apply only `6f311498`'s 20**. **Handlers:** `handle_workspace_move` (`src/app/api/workspaces.rs:114`) captures the public id BEFORE the move, emits `WorkspaceMoved` (`:139`) only when the state actually reordered, and answers from the same `workspaces` vector it published; `handle_tab_move` (`src/app/api/tabs.rs:170`) does the same for `TabMoved` (`:203`); `AppState::move_workspace` (`src/app/actions.rs:1188`) now returns `bool` and short-circuits a no-op move (`source_idx == target_idx`) BEFORE `mark_session_dirty`, so a drag that lands where it started neither dirties the session nor emits an event — upstream's `.min(self.workspaces.len())` clamp is dropped because the pre-checked `insert_idx <= len` already bounds `target_idx`. Two private helpers remove the duplicated list building: `App::workspace_list_info` (`src/app/api/workspaces.rs:170`) and `App::tab_list_info` (`src/app/api/tabs.rs:256`), each now serving both the `list` handler and the `move` handler. `plugin_context_for_event` (`src/app/api/plugins/context.rs:57`, `:80`) routes the two new `EventData` variants onto the existing workspace/tab context arms — an exhaustive match, so the compiler forced this. **Adaptations vs upstream:** (1) upstream's `src/api/schema/tests.rs` does not exist here — its event and subscription round trips are retargeted into `src/api/schema.rs` `mod tests`, following M5-01; (2) upstream folds the `LayoutSplitRatioSet` response round trip into `layout_export_apply_round_trip`, which this fork does not have, so it lands as a dedicated `layout_split_ratio_set_response_round_trips` (`src/api/schema.rs:534`); (3) upstream's `KNOWN_EVENT_KINDS` const does not exist in this fork, so its two `+` lines have no target — `PLUGIN_HOOK_EVENT_KINDS` is the fork's only event-kind list and both new kinds are added there; (4) upstream's `EventKind` carries `WorktreeCreated`/`WorktreeOpened`/`WorktreeRemoved`, which this fork never ported, so the fork's list runs 17 -> 19 where upstream's runs 20 -> 22; (5) `tests/cli_wrapper.rs` is kept MINIMAL (six `14` -> `15` literals, no `CURRENT_PROTOCOL` import) because upstream deletes that file in a later milestone; (6) the identity renames in the ported test bodies (`register_spawned_zynk_pid`, `cleanup_spawned_zynk`, `.zynk-test-owner-pid`). **Invariants checked:** the ordered `Method` wire-id list and `METHOD_WIRE_IDS` are byte-identical — this commit changes no method id and `zynk.message_received` still routes; there are no `zynk.*` `EventKind`s to preserve (the conversation layer rides `Method`, not events); fork-owned `src/app/api/zynk.rs`, `src/zynk/**`, `src/cli/**` and `src/cli.rs` are untouched; `src/app/state.rs`, `src/pane/state.rs` and `src/ui.rs` are untouched, so `AppState`/`PaneState` stay pure data and `render(&AppState, frame)` gained no mutation; `src/app/creation.rs` is untouched, so `authoritative_receiver_identity` is byte-identical (32 lines, sha256 `9430e4c6ec415e59…`); the `AppEvent::HookStateReported` identity region in `src/app/actions.rs` is byte-identical against `e43db7b` (81-line window, sha256 `145b9ecd18ed1b50…`) — the whole `actions.rs` diff is confined to `move_workspace`. **Tests (RED first):** the new fork-only characterization test `event_kind_wire_ids_are_stable` (`src/api/schema.rs:927`) pins the FULL ordered `EventKind` contract — all 19 variants as `(variant, serde id, dot name)` triples in `EVENT_KIND_WIRE_IDS` (`:851`) — against the id list `serde` itself reports for an unknown tag, so a rename, reorder, addition or removal fails it; it also asserts `PLUGIN_HOOK_EVENT_KINDS` covers every kind except `pane.output_changed`, so a new event kind cannot silently skip the plugin surface. RED at `e43db7b`: 2 compile errors (`error[E0599]` for the missing `EventKind::WorkspaceMoved` and `EventKind::TabMoved`), `cargo test --no-run` exit 101. Plus the mirrored schema tests — `event_envelope_round_trips` (`:406`) now round trips all three envelopes, `layout_split_ratio_set_response_round_trips` (`:534`) asserts the `"type":"layout_split_ratio_set"` tag, `authority_mutation_requests_round_trip` (`:1024`) gains the `workspace.moved` + `tab.moved` subscribe round trip — and the ported handler tests `api_tab_move_reorders_tabs_in_target_workspace` / `api_workspace_move_reorders_workspaces` (both now assert the emitted event off a shared `EventHub`) and the new `api_workspace_move_noop_does_not_emit_event` (`src/app/api/workspaces.rs:273`). **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`/`ZYNK_*`/`XDG_*`/`TMPDIR`):** `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` exit 0; `--bin zynk -E 'test(/api::schema/)'` 32/32 (30 -> 32), `-E 'test(/protocol::wire/)'` 47/47 under the renamed test, `-E 'test(/zynk::header/)'` 28/28, `-E 'test(/zynk::receipt/)'` 29/29, `-E 'test(/app::/)'` 604/604 (603 -> 604), the delivery-transition + body-purity set 51/51; `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 52/52; the adapted integration suites `--test api_ping --test client_mode --test cross_area --test detach_reattach --test multi_client --test server_headless --test cli_wrapper` 154/154 passed, 1 skipped; the full `--bin zynk` suite 2715/2715 passed, 1 skipped (2712 -> 2715, +3 = the three new tests); per-file `#[test]`+`#[tokio::test]` counts show no decrease and `src/` totals 2713 -> 2716; `just gate` clean (tracked-path 1693 files, scrub 82 files, gitleaks no leaks). |
| *(this commit)* | `97f78226` `9a91a2ac` *(both pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **Refactor (single mutation path): every TUI modal — rename, resize, confirm-close, context menu, the three worktree dialogs and the tab-bar wheel — now reaches state through the same API handler a socket client calls, and every such call goes through one named dispatch seam.** Third and fourth links of the M5 chain, on top of the `1a4e94e5` seam and the `8b74fb8a` placement events. **`97f78226` (route modal mutations through API authority):** key handling in `src/app/mod.rs:1597` (`route_client_input`) stops calling the free `input::handle_rename_key` / `handle_resize_key` / `handle_confirm_close_key` / `handle_context_menu_key` and calls the `App` methods `handle_rename_key_via_api` / `handle_resize_key_via_api` / `handle_confirm_close_key_via_api` / `handle_context_menu_key_via_api` instead. Mouse handling stops mutating inline: `AppState::handle_mouse` (`src/app/input/mouse.rs`) gains three `MouseAction` variants — `RenameModal(ModalAction)` (`:54`), `ConfirmCloseAccept` (`:55`), `ContextMenu { menu, idx }` (`:56`) — and returns them (`:243`, `:406`, `:413`) instead of calling `apply_rename_action` / `confirm_close_accept` / `apply_context_menu_action`, so the App layer at `src/app/input/mod.rs:323` dispatches them onto `apply_rename_mouse_action_via_api` / `confirm_close_accept_via_api` / `apply_context_menu_action_via_api`. The tab-bar wheel (`src/app/input/mouse.rs:905`) stops calling `AppState::previous_tab` / `next_tab` and computes the wrapped target index, returning `MouseAction::FocusTab` so a scroll emits `tab.focused` like every other focus change. Two new adapters in `src/app/input/modal.rs` remove the duplicated bodies: `apply_rename_mouse_action_via_api` (`:1013`) is now the one place the keyboard and the mouse turn a `ModalAction` into a `workspace.rename` / `tab.create` / `tab.rename` / `pane.rename` dispatch, and `confirm_close_accept_via_api` (`:1025`) is the one place a confirm-close accept becomes `workspace.close`; `handle_rename_key_via_api` (`:917`) and `handle_confirm_close_key_via_api` (`:1071`) both call them. `handle_rename_key` splits: the text-editing half becomes the live `handle_rename_edit_key` (`:594`) that both paths share, while the action half stays as the test-only `handle_rename_key`. Consequently the free direct-mutation functions have no production caller left and are gated `#[cfg(test)]` as characterization targets — `apply_rename_action`, `handle_rename_key`, `handle_resize_key`, `confirm_close_accept`, `handle_confirm_close_key`, `apply_context_menu_action`, `handle_context_menu_key` (`src/app/input/modal.rs`), `AppState::resize_pane` (`src/app/actions.rs:1587`), and in `src/app/worktrees.rs` `open_selected_existing_worktree`, `mark_opened_existing_worktree_membership`, `start_worktree_add`, `start_worktree_remove`; `src/app/input/mod.rs:46` drops the four names from its `pub(crate) use`, and the `Direction` (`src/app/input/modal.rs:3`) and `AppEvent` (`src/app/worktrees.rs:11`) imports become `#[cfg(test)]` with them. The three worktree dialogs submit through the API on Enter: `handle_worktree_create_key` (`:250`), `handle_worktree_open_key` (`:329`) and `handle_worktree_remove_key` (`:612`) now call `submit_worktree_create_via_api` / `submit_worktree_open_via_api` / `submit_worktree_remove_via_api`, and the headless deferred-submit block (`src/server/headless.rs:498`, `:506`, `:514`) is renamed onto the same three, which is the whole of that file's diff. **`9a91a2ac` (route TUI mutations through runtime dispatch):** the new fork-owned-adjacent module `src/app/runtime_mutations.rs` is the single named seam every later chain commit extends — `dispatch_tui_runtime_mutation` / `dispatch_tui_deferred_runtime_mutation` (thin wrappers over `dispatch_tui_api_request` / `dispatch_tui_deferred_api_request`) plus six typed helpers `runtime_workspace_focus` / `runtime_workspace_close` / `runtime_tab_focus` / `runtime_tab_close` / `runtime_pane_focus` / `runtime_pane_close` that build the `Method` themselves; it is ported VERBATIM apart from the module declaration (`src/app/mod.rs:17`) so the later diffs in the chain apply cleanly. Every TUI mutation call site is renamed onto it: 7 in `src/app/input/modal.rs`, 10 in `src/app/input/navigate.rs` plus 6 replaced outright by the typed helpers, 3 in `src/app/mod.rs` (`run`), 3 in `src/app/worktrees.rs`. The single deliberate hold-out is `tui.server.reload_config` (`src/app/input/navigate.rs:328`), which stays on `dispatch_tui_api_request` exactly as upstream leaves it — it is a server command, not a state mutation. **Adaptations vs upstream:** (1) upstream's one-line `src/app/api/worktrees.rs` hunk (`#[cfg(test)]` on `emit_worktree_opened_for_workspace`) has NO TARGET here — this fork never ported the `WorktreeCreated`/`WorktreeOpened`/`WorktreeRemoved` event family (recorded in the `8b74fb8a` row above), so no such function exists and the file is left untouched; (2) for the same reason the new `worktree_open_enter_submits_through_api_path` test asserts the three events this fork actually emits (`WorkspaceCreated`, `TabCreated`, `PaneCreated`) where upstream asserts four; (3) this fork lacks upstream's `app_for_worktree_tests_with_event_hub` / `event_kinds` / `shutdown_test_runtimes` test helpers, so they are added alongside the test that needs them and `app_for_worktree_tests` is re-expressed in terms of the new one; (4) the identity renames in the ported test bodies (`zynk` repo/branch labels, `issue/195`). **Invariants checked:** `src/app/state.rs`, `src/pane/state.rs` and `src/ui.rs` are untouched, so `AppState`/`PaneState` stay pure data and `render(&AppState, frame)` gained no mutation — the new module's own test proves the boundary by driving `runtime_workspace_focus` + `runtime_workspace_close` against an `AppState::test_new()` with zero terminal runtimes; fork-owned `src/app/api/zynk.rs`, `src/zynk/**`, `src/cli/**` and `src/cli.rs` are untouched; the `Method` wire-id list is unchanged (`method_wire_ids_are_stable` green, same 80 ids) and this commit adds no method and no event kind (`event_kind_wire_ids_are_stable` green); the fork's receipt + embedding worker install in `src/server/headless.rs` is intact — `install_db_workers` is still defined (`:3836`) and still called at `:1064`, `:3704` and `:3913`, with the `zynk_receipt_worker` / `zynk_embedding_worker` pause, drain and teardown sites at `:1028`, `:1035`, `:1042`, `:1045`, `:1056`, `:1057` and `:3521` byte-for-byte unchanged; `src/app/creation.rs` is untouched, so `authoritative_receiver_identity` is byte-identical (32 lines, sha256 `9430e4c6ec415e59…`); the `AppEvent::HookStateReported` identity region of `src/app/actions.rs` is byte-identical (81-line window, sha256 `145b9ecd18ed1b50…`), the whole `actions.rs` diff being the one `#[cfg(test)]` attribute on `resize_pane`. **Tests (RED first):** the new fork-only purity test `runtime_workspace_mutations_drive_pure_state_without_a_pty` (`src/app/runtime_mutations.rs:84`) documents the boundary the chain establishes — RED at `7df7be4` with 2 × `error[E0599]` (`no method named runtime_workspace_focus` / `runtime_workspace_close` found for struct `app::App`), `cargo test --no-run` exit 101, because the dispatch module does not exist.
